# Module index

Generated by `node scripts/gen-docs.mjs` from `docs/modules.json`, `docs/globals.json`,
`tests/suites/*.json`, the import graph and the sources themselves. Do not edit it:
`tests/docs-contract.mjs` regenerates it and fails on any difference. The frozen
system map is `docs/map-2026-10-05.md`; it is a snapshot, and `node scripts/map/audit.mjs`
reports how many of its `path:line` references still resolve.

1126 files in 293 rows. A file belongs to the most specific row that names it (an exact path, else the glob with the most literal characters).

The suites column lists the suites whose import closure reaches the file or whose `covers`
in `tests/suites/<name>.json` name it, from those files' own fields; `**` covers are left out.

## Domains

- [site](#site): The landing page, articles and site-wide assets (42 files)
- [lab](#lab): The Econometrics Lab: courses, review, placement, projects and their storage (133 files)
- [worker](#worker): The Worker, its configuration, schema and migrations (57 files)
- [flows-server](#flows-server): Flows logic shared by the Worker, the pipeline and the browser bundles (259 files)
- [flows-rail](#flows-rail): The real-time rail (5 files)
- [flows-pipeline](#flows-pipeline): The nightly pipeline, the live leg and their tools (59 files)
- [flows-client](#flows-client): Flows pages, scripts and styles (129 files)
- [tooling](#tooling): Generators and authoring tools (31 files)
- [tests](#tests): Suites, helpers and fixtures (387 files)
- [ci](#ci): GitHub Actions workflows (13 files)
- [config](#config): Repository configuration (3 files)
- [docs](#docs): Documents and the generated index (8 files)

### `shared/markets.js`, `assets/js/market-ticker.js`

The landing index ticker: the Worker's Yahoo parse (the change is against the previous session's close taken from the same response's dated bars, never `chartPreviousClose`; each quote keeps its own `asOf`, `asOfDay`, `prevClose`, `prevDay` and, when the response names the session of the quote's own day, `sessionEnd`) and the strip that prints each quote's own İstanbul time, `Close <weekday>` for a closed market (a close is a quote struck within five minutes of the exchange's `sessionEnd`; the lag of the quote behind the fetch decides only where there is none), and a dash for a quote with no base.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `assets/js/market-ticker.js` |  | market-ticker |
| `shared/markets.js` | `shared/markets.js#MARKET_CRON_STALE_MS`, `shared/markets.js#MARKET_INDICES`, `shared/markets.js#MARKET_STALE_MS`, `shared/markets.js#buildSnapshot`, `shared/markets.js#marketRefreshDue`, `shared/markets.js#parseIndexQuote` | flows, flows-basis, flows-desk-audit, flows-dossier-reads, flows-engine-budget, flows-freshness and 10 more |

### `tests/film/**`, `assets/img/flows-walkthrough-v2.*`, `tests/flows-film-contract.mjs`

The Flows walkthrough film (v2) on the public About page. `capture.mjs` renders the real pages from the test fixtures at 1920x1080 on a virtual clock (15 fps, JPEG frames, every frame carrying a Sample data ribbon, scenes that cannot open are skipped and named in `manifest.json`, `--only 5,7` re-captures chosen scenes); `assemble.mjs` renders the title and end cards, joins the scenes with crossfades through ffmpeg into `flows-walkthrough-v2.mp4` (H.264, under 8 MB), its poster and English WebVTT captions, and checks the result with ffprobe. The About page plays it with controls, no autoplay and `preload="none"`; `flows-film` holds the markup, the sample-data caption and the file sizes. Not generated in CI: the committed files are the product, regenerated by hand with `node tests/film/capture.mjs --out <dir>` then `node tests/film/assemble.mjs --frames <dir> --out assets/img`.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `assets/img/flows-walkthrough-v2.jpg` |  | flows-film |
| `assets/img/flows-walkthrough-v2.mp4` |  | flows-film |
| `assets/img/flows-walkthrough-v2.vtt` |  | flows-film |
| `tests/film/assemble.mjs` | `tests/film/assemble.mjs#assembleFilm` | flows-film |
| `tests/film/capture.mjs` | `tests/film/capture.mjs#captureScenes` | flows-film |
| `tests/flows-film-contract.mjs` |  | flows-film |

### `schema.sql`

D1 `users`, `progress`, `stats`, `mastery`, idempotent `mastery_attempts`, minimal `placement`, and per-owner `learning_sync` generation tables; the Flows tables, including `flows_live` (only `live:*` ids), `flows_tape`, `flows_clock`, the trigger that makes dated archive rows immutable and the two triggers that make `ideas:<date>` and `ideas-out:<date>` rows (and their `:r<n>` revisions) neither updatable nor deletable, with `shared/flows-archive.js` holding the write-once decision, its refusals (`archive_permanent` for these keys) and `PERMANENT_ARCHIVE_KEY_RE`; the model spend (`flows_ai_usage`, `flows_ai_usage_model`), board summary and Neuron (`flows_ai_summary`, `flows_neuron`) tables. `tests/academy-contract.mjs` builds three `node:sqlite` databases (this file, every `migrations/*.sql` in number order, the Worker's first-use DDL) and holds their tables, columns by name, CHECK clauses (literals compared verbatim), indexes, triggers and views equal; only `users`, `progress` and `stats` have no first-use DDL. Every `CREATE` and `DROP` of a table, index, trigger or view in `worker.js` and `shared/`, whatever its case or spacing, must sit inside a top-level DDL constant the suite evaluates, so inline DDL cannot escape the comparison. Every `ALTER TABLE` in `worker.js` and `shared/` must be one of the sites the suite lists, and each column it adds must be declared with the same type in this file and the migrations.

### `server/http.js`, `server/errors.js`, `server/log.js`, `shared/flows-log.js`, `tests/server-kernel.mjs`, `tests/server-log.mjs`

The Worker-only HTTP kernel. `http.js`: `HttpError` (with `HttpError.of(code, message?, headers?, details?)`), `json`, `apiError`, `redirect`, `requireMethod`, `requireSameOrigin`, `requireMutationOwner`, `requireReadOwnerIfPresent`, `readBounded`, `readJSON`, `tickerParam`, `requireTicker`, `keepAlive`, `errorText`, `internalKey(namespace, path)` (the `https://flows-<namespace>.internal/<path>` Cache API keys) and `edgeCache` (`handle`, `get`, `put`, each failure a miss). `errors.js`: `ERRORS`, one frozen `[status, default message]` per code. `log.js`: `logFailure(level, event, fields, error)`, the one way the Worker writes an unexpected failure. The shape is built by `shared/flows-log.js`, a leaf the shared modules can import: `failureEntry`, `failureLine` and `emitFailure(sink, level, event, fields, error)` write one JSON line `{ event, message, level, route, code, ...fields, error }`, with `message` an alias of `event` for one release, `level` `warn` or `error`, `route` the caller's route or path, `code` the caller's or the error's own string code, `error` the text of the caught value or null, and reserved keys that a field cannot rewrite. Logging never throws (an unserialisable field falls back to a minimal line, a sink that fails is ignored). The Worker's sites, the live layer's injected `log` sinks, the reading, the AI failover and the rail's one-a-minute log all go through it; success is never logged or sampled, so it adds no events. `tests/server-log.mjs` holds the shape, drives a real site of each module, and scans the closure for a hand-built failure line. `server/` holds no top-level mutable binding, no comments and is excluded from the static bundle. The suite runs every helper against a verbatim copy of the helper it replaced over tables of requests, bodies, origins and tickers, holds every literal `new HttpError(status, "code"` in `worker.js` and `shared/` to the table's status, and holds the failure-log key order. Literal `new HttpError(` sites stay where they are (the ingest scans pin some); a module moves to `HttpError.of` with its own move.

### `server/router.js`, `server/routes/flows-read.js`, `tests/server-router.mjs`

The Worker's route table, consulted first by `route()` and falling through to the remaining `if` chain. `createRouter(...families)` validates each row `{ id, path, methods, auth, handler }` (an absolute path and an id declared once, a non-empty list of HTTP methods or the single wildcard `["*"]` for a row whose handler owns its method policy, `auth` of `none` or `flows`, a handler), freezes it, and `handle(rc, gates)` returns `null` for a path with no row, else runs the method gate, then the session gate (`gates.flows`, a missing session being the 401 `unauthorized`), then the handler with `{ request, env, url, ctx, session }`: the order the Flows prefix block always had. `flowsReadRows(deps)` is the first family, 23 `GET` rows behind the Flows session: `board`, `brief`, `card`, `card-x`, `hist`, the overlaid `flowalerts`, `pulse` and `news`, and the fifteen plain nightly reads built by one `nightly(key, pending)` factory (`market`, `events`, `scoretrack`, `meta`, `political`, `unusual`, `movers`, `sectors`, `sector-premium`, `universe`, `regime`, `ideas`, `focus`, `roster`, `record`). The worker-local readers it needs (`readServed`, `readFlowsPayload`, `readWithOverlay`, `passthrough`, `recallLastGood`, `storeGone`, `absentKey`, `cardWithEngine`, `briefWithLive`, `nightlyFreshHeaders`) are injected, so `server/` imports nothing from `worker.js` or `cloudflare:`. A new route in this family is a row, never a new `if`; the ceiling enumeration in `flows-reads-contract` takes the table's paths plus the literals still in the chain (at least thirty together) and fails for any without a rows-read ceiling. The suite runs every row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over eight stored-row worlds and seven store behaviours, comparing status, headers, body, errors and the order of calls.

### `server/routes/flows-desk.js`, `tests/server-desk.mjs`

The flows-desk family in the route table: `flowsDeskRows(deps)` is three `GET` rows behind the Flows session, `chain` (keyed by ticker, strategy and rank, 120 rows), `strategy` (the context, or one expiry with or without the engine; a malformed expiry is `400 invalid_expiry` before any gate) and `live` (the stock-state quote). Each handler builds the member's `vendorGate` once and hands the Worker's single-flight `serveCachedVendorRead` (or `quoteResponse`) its key, gate and builder; the builders, the gate and the reader are injected and stay in `worker.js`, so the family names no limiter and makes no vendor call itself. `isRealDate` (a real calendar date in 2000 to 2100, so `2026-02-30` is `400 invalid_expiry` before any gate, cache key or vendor call) and `RANK_KEYS` are read by the handlers, not the Worker. The suite runs every row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over the parameter grid (tickers, strategies, ranks, expiries, engine and refresh flags, two members), comparing status, headers, body, errors and the order of calls, and holds the 405-before-401 gate order. `tape`, `now` and `lk` stay in the chain for the live-layer family.

### `server/routes/flows-ai.js`, `tests/server-ai.mjs`

The flows-ai family in the route table: `flowsAiRows(deps)` is four rows behind the Flows session, `ai-usage`, `summary` and `dossier` by `GET` and `ask` by `POST`. The handlers are the route bodies only: the board summary or a `summaryResponse` for a ticker (a malformed ticker is `400 invalid_ticker`), the dossier behind `ensureFlowsTables`, and the Ask box (same-origin check, the question, the `AI_ASK` flood brake with its `Retry-After`, the stored briefing with its live overlay, then `askAnswer`). Everything that reaches a model stays in `worker.js` behind `cappedAi` and `meteredAi` and is injected (`askSpend`, `summaryResponse`, `readFlowsSummary`, `neuronProvenance`, `dossierResponse`, `askQuestion`, `askAnswer`, `readFlowsPayload`, `briefWithLive`, and `ASK_FLOOD_PERIOD_S`, which the contract reads off the Worker); the module names no model binding, and `checkModelCalls` still reads it as part of `server/`. The prefix block's method gate is `GET` alone now that `ask` is a `POST` row. The suite runs every row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over the parameter grid (tickers, board summaries, stored briefings, origins, limiter behaviours, three callers) and holds the 405-before-401 gate order.

### `server/routes/flows-ingest.js`, `server/store.js`, `server/schema.js`, `tests/server-ingest.mjs`

The flows-ingest family, the store and the schema registry. `flowsIngestRows(deps)` is one row, `/api/flows/ingest` by `GET`, `POST` and `DELETE`, whose credential is its own bearer token (the nightly, the static live token on a loopback host, or the live OIDC token) and not the Flows session; the method gate, the credential checks and the key and scope rules run in the order the branch had them. `server/store.js` is the only module that holds `flows_payload` SQL: `createFlowsStore({ ensureFlowsTables })` has `read`, `metadata`, `listing`, `remove`, `createArchive` and `write` (the nightly's `meta` lands its ledger row in the same batch; its upsert sets `chash`, `session`, `quality`, `epoch` and `asof` back to NULL and zero and bumps `rev`, so a row the v1 writer replaced never carries metadata that describes other bytes), and the door's `doorRead`, `doorWrite`, `doorBatch`, `have`, `planWrite`, `planRead` and `quarantine`, and exports `storedFrom`, `ingestKeyParts`, `INGEST_VIEW_KEY_RE` and the listing limits; `worker.js` reads the nightly rows through the same store. `server/schema.js` is the registry of the Flows statements, `{ name, table, ddl, addedColumns, migration, owner }`: the twelve the Worker has always sent (built from the DDL each domain exports plus the six tables that were literals in `worker.js`, in the order it sent them), `flows_ai_outcome` and `flows_ai_reject` after them, then `flows_quarantine` and the three `flows_payload_manifest_*` triggers; `FLOWS_SCHEMA_SQL` is derived from it. `upgradeColumns(db, table, added, known?)` replaces `upgradeClockColumns` for every table: it adds only the columns a table lacks, tolerates `duplicate column`, rethrows anything else, and takes names and types only in the shapes `checkAdded` allows. `applySchema(db, registry)` is the bootstrap `ensureFlowsTables` runs: one batch of the CREATEs and one `PRAGMA table_info` per table that has added columns (today `flows_payload` and `flows_clock`, so twenty-two statements), then the upgrades. A new column on a registered table is one entry in its `addedColumns` list (`PAYLOAD_ADDED_COLUMNS`, `CLOCK_ADDED_COLUMNS`, `LEDGER_ADDED_COLUMNS`, which is empty today) plus the same column in the table's DDL, `schema.sql` and a migration, and it is added at first use instead of being refused by `batchWithLedger`. The suite runs the row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over 7 credentials, 6 methods, 39 queries and 5 bodies (statements, call order, errors, bodies and the rows left behind), checks the store, the registry against the migrations and `schema.sql` (twenty statements), `upgradeColumns` against hostile names, races and refused batches, and the Worker scans. The door itself is `server/door.js` and `shared/flows-door.js`, below.

### `shared/flows-door.js`, `server/door.js`, `tests/flows-door-v2.mjs`

The ingest door v2 in warn mode (B3a and B3), behind `FLOWS_DOOR_V2` (`wrangler.toml` [vars], shipped `warn` since 2026-10-24; only the exact word `warn` turns it on, so `enforce` is `off` until B3b). With the switch off the ingest route is byte for byte what it was, and `?op=` and `X-Content-Hash` are ignored. With `warn`, a non-archive, non-live write is inspected, its row gets metadata, and nothing is refused. `shared/flows-door.js` is the pure leaf (it imports `flows-live.js` and `flows-freshness.js` only): `canonicalize` (keys sorted; the volatile stamps `generatedAt`, `readAt`, `screenerReadAt`, `refreshedAt`, `producer` and `fresh.readAt` dropped; non-finite numbers and nesting past 64 levels named), `sha256Hex`, `inspect(key, text, { now })` (per-product structural findings derived from the dry-run emit, every payload of which passes with none: `not_object`, `missing_key`, `schema_version`, `depth`, `panel_shape`, `ticker_grammar`, `ticker_mismatch`, `session_format`, `instant_format`, `session_future` against the next session the calendar expects, `n_mismatch`, `column_length`, `units_missing`, `rows_type`, `row_shape`; range violations in the universe are flags that cost quality and are never structural; a pending placeholder has no required keys), the ordering tuple `(epoch, session, quality, asof)` with `quality = 4 x round(63 x ok base panels / base panels) + tier`, so a complete L1 (253) beats an L2 that misses a base panel (235) and a complete L2 (255) beats a complete L1, `casVerdict` (`new`; `unchanged` by canonical hash; `advance` on an equal or higher tuple; `superseded`), `regressionOf` (a later session's card that keeps under three quarters of the stored base panels), `assess` (adds `size_collapse` under 40 per cent of the stored size for the products whose size follows the universe, `stale_producer` and `hash_mismatch`), `membershipFlag`, `planOf` and `doorMode`. `server/door.js` is `createDoor({ store, ensureFlowsTables, storeGone, log })`: `write` (one batch reading the key's stored tuple and, for a ticker key, the universe's name list, then one write; the response gains `door { mode, chash, product, session, quality, epoch, asof, cas, would, flags, ring }`), and `operation` for `?op=batch` (up to 100 keys or 2 MiB, each judged on its own; archive keys, `live:` keys, `meta` and duplicates refused per item; writes in D1 batches of 16 that are bisected to the poison key when the store refuses one deterministically, and not when the failure looks transient, at most forty failed batches), `?op=have` (up to 90 `(key, chash)` pairs in one statement; answers `have` and `need`) and `?op=plan` (the nightly's planned card set for a session, kept as the row `plan`; an older session never replaces a newer one). Every would-have-refused finding lands in `flows_quarantine`, a ring of 200 rows (id is the position, pruned by id range; bodies of at most 64 KiB kept for the newest twenty; a per-producer brake that keeps no body after twenty events of one check in the newest forty). The regression guard only logs its count (`door would hold regressions`); a ring that cannot be written is logged and never fails an ingest. `X-Producer-Id` names the producer and `X-Producer-Epoch` declares its epoch. The enforcing compare-and-set SQL exists in the store (`doorWrite` with `enforce`) and is proved equal to `casVerdict` over every pair of 36 tuples, but no route passes it. The synthetic and fixture refusal is another row's hook and is not here. `tests/flows-door-v2.mjs` reads the dry-run emit and runs the leaf, the store and the route over the warn paths, the batch, `have` and `plan`, the off paths, the upgrade of an old database, the manifest triggers and the round trip off, warn, off, warn with row-content assertions, and plants twenty-one wrong builds that its batteries must fail.

### `server/routes/flows-film.js`

The walkthrough film route, `GET` and `HEAD` `/flows/about/film.mp4` with `auth: none`: the static asset layer ignores `Range` and Safari will not play an mp4 without a 206, so the row reads the committed file through `env.ASSETS`, answers `Accept-Ranges: bytes`, a 206 with `Content-Range` for a satisfiable range (open-ended and suffix forms too), a 416 for one past the end and the Flows 404 when `FLOWS_FRONT_DOOR` is `off`. `tests/flows-film-contract.mjs` drives it.

### `server/routes/flows-pages.js`, `tests/server-pages.mjs`

The flows-pages family in the route table: `flowsPageRows(deps)` is the sign-in page, `/flows`, the fourteen gated pages (`/flows/`, `long`, `short`, `watch`, `market`, `history`, `desk`, `strategy`, `ask`, `ticker`, `unusual`, `events`, `track`, `political`) and a 308 row for the bare form of each page but Home, all `GET` and `HEAD` with `auth: none`, because an anonymous visitor is answered with the sign-in page and HTTP 200, never a 401. The page list is one array, and the bare redirects and the `?t=` reader redirect (a 302 to the ticker page from Home, `long`, `short` and `watch`, before any session is read) are generated from it. A page row reads the session through the injected `currentFlowsUser`, for Home alone the board summary through `readFlowsSummary`, and then asks the injected `isOwner(env, username)` whether the member is an owner, which `market` and `unusual` take as `owner`. The public front door is two more rows with a 308 row for each bare form: `/flows/about/` and `/flows/glossary/`, answered to a signed-out reader with no session read, and the Flows 404 when `FLOWS_FRONT_DOOR` is `off` (a method other than `GET` and `HEAD` is the row's 405 first, where the chain answered 404 while the front door was off). The page functions are `shared/flows-pages.js`'s, and the family reads no store and names no vendor or model. `POST /flows/login` and `/flows/logout` stay in the chain for the auth family. The suite runs every row against a verbatim copy of the branch it replaced over every path, query, method, member and front-door setting (84,000 checks) and holds the Worker's table, scans and the absence of the old branches.

### `server/routes/flows-rt.js`, `tests/server-rt.mjs`

The rail family in the route table: `flowsRtRows({ serveRt })` is three rows, `/api/rt/ws`, `/api/rt/snap` and `/api/rt/status` (the paths are `RT_PATHS`, declared beside the handler in `shared/flows-rt-routes.js`), each with `methods: ["*"]` and `auth: "none"`. The rail answers its kill switch before it looks at the method or the session, so a mistyped, absent or off `FLOWS_RT_MODE` is a 404 `rt_off` even to an anonymous `OPTIONS`, and only then does it take the session (401), the method (405 naming GET), the audience (403) and the topic list (400); the router therefore gates neither, and the row hands the request to `serveRt` bound in `worker.js` with `json`, `HttpError`, `requireSameOrigin` and the session reader. The prefix branch stays in the chain for every other path under `/api/rt/`, through the same bound handler, so an unknown path is still the kill switch's 404 or a JSON `not_found`. The suite compares the rows with the branch they replaced over 6,000 requests (every path, query, method, environment with the rail on, off, unset, without its binding and for members, caller and upgrade-header set; status, headers, body, the error and the session reads) and scans the Worker for the single bound call.

### `tests/flows-desk-audit.mjs`, `tests/flows-desk-audit-render.mjs`

The Premium desk audit's regression suites. `flows-desk-audit` (Node, no server) drives `worker.js` against stubbed vendor bodies: a missing, zero, negative or unreadable `prev_close` is `null` on the chain and strategy routes and never the spot (the Strategy hero prints a dash with its reason instead of a flat +0.00); the strategy route's engine (`engine=1`) prices off the regular close after the bell, before the open and on a weekend, reading the bars once and only when the state is not a regular print, and says it is unavailable when no regular price exists; a report that precedes every possible sale (`earnings.precedesSale`) leaves every line `null`, not `crosses`. Each is held by planted Worker builds loaded through `tests/truth/mutants.mjs`. `flows-desk-audit-render` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds: no late response for the old side in the list after a Puts to Calls switch (a request token per entry, invalidated by `setSide`), a kept copy served under the rate limit saying so with its own read time in TRT and not promising that Refresh requotes it, the earnings line per symbol (passed date, report before the next sale, clear, undetermined, crossing), the best deployment being the line that earns the most time value (cash in shown beside it, the in-the-money part named as not income), the short-call `Premium ÷ collateral` rank asking the server for `yieldOnCollateral`, the Forward fact of each line and the cushion copy, no signed zero, one answer under six process time zones, the WCAG 2.5.8 spacing of every undersized target at four widths and axe clean on the populated desk at 390 and 1440. Each is held by planted wrong builds of the served script.

## flows-server

Flows logic shared by the Worker, the pipeline and the browser bundles.

### `shared/flows-ai-broker.js`, `server/ai.js`, `tests/flows-ai-broker.mjs`

The AI broker and its outcome telemetry. `shared/flows-ai-broker.js` is pure: the four surfaces (`board`, `neuron`, `ask`, `read`), the fourteen outcomes (`clean`, `trimmed`, `refused`, `unparsable`, `overlong`, `empty`, `length`, `budget`, `class`, `user`, `allowance`, `capacity`, `plan`, `unreachable`), `chainFor`, `transportOutcome` (a reply with no text), `guardOutcome` (the stored `llm` and `guard` of a surface mapped to an outcome and a low-cardinality reason), `tokenOf` (a culprit, 40 characters, no control or bidi characters) and `foldCounters` (the owner block's shape: per surface and model, calls, outcomes, the share of calls that reached a model whose wording failed, mean and max latency, and the fifty latest refusals). `server/ai.js` is the I/O: `aiCall(env, deps, { surface, chain, messages, opts, onUsage })` runs `askModels(deps.ai(), ...)`, times it, settles a reply with no text itself and returns the reply with `call.settle(verdict, culprit)` for the surface to call once it has judged the wording (a second settle does nothing). A settle upserts one `flows_ai_outcome` row (day, surface, model, outcome, reason: `n`, `ms_sum`, `ms_max`) and, for a wording failure with a culprit, writes `flows_ai_reject (surface, slot 0..49)` in the same batch; it never throws, and a store that refuses it is logged once (`ai outcome not recorded`). `readAiBlock(env, deps, { days })` is the owner block in one trip (today's counters; up to seven days behind `days`) and `pruneAiOutcomes` keeps eight days, run in the 03:00 ET window. The block is mounted by the health and owner-view row (P1-27). Nothing in it reads the binding or runs a model: `shared/flows-ai.js` stays the only place. The suite covers the outcome table, the fold, `aiCall` against a scripted binding and a counting D1 (one trip a call, two rows for a refusal with a culprit, a ring that wraps at fifty, a refused or missing store, the budget refusal counted without the model asked), the block's trips and rows, retention, the registry and migration 0017 parity, and the scans.

### `shared/flows-evt.js`, `tests/flows-evt.mjs`, `tests/lib/evt-measure.mjs`, `tests/gen-moves-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-moves-*.json`

The extreme-value leaf (F-21): no imports. The generalised Pareto fit by a profile likelihood in the shape (`betaHat` solves the scale for each shape, `fitGpd` minimises over it with Brent's method), the 90% and 95% profile-likelihood interval (`profileInterval`), survival and inverse survival, and the pooled shape of exceedances over each name's own threshold with the name-level scale set to its mean excess times (1 - xi) (`pooledShape`, `pooledInterval`, `exceedances`, `exceedanceGroups`). The suite holds the fit to scipy on committed reference samples (`gen-moves-fixtures.py` writes `fixtures-moves-gpd.json` with the generator's hash), the one-name profile interval to 88 to 92% coverage over 2,000 samples, the pooled shape to within 0.02 of the truth at 3,200 exceedances with spread scales (an inverse-variance weighted mean of per-name shapes lands outside that band), and publishes in `fixtures-moves-bands.json` what every later window rests on: the production-class true model's observed over predicted exceedances at 3 to 6 standard deviations for one seed and for ten, and the pooled interval's coverage on the dependent daily world (`w1dWorld`, with sector factors, common volatility shocks and per-name scales) at 400 seeds beside the independent-names figures. The pooled shape's variance under dependence by date: `pooledClustered` (the score of each exceedance with the scale moving with the shape, plus the correction for the estimated mean excess, summed by day and squared over the squared curvature), `pooledJackknife` (delete-a-block over six blocks of days with every threshold re-estimated, a t interval on blocks - 1 degrees of freedom, `tCritical95`) and `dateBootstrapShape` (blocks of days resampled with replacement, seeded), the first two held to an independent numpy implementation with numerical derivatives (`fixtures-moves-cluster.json`). On the dependent world the unclustered interval covers 88%, 84%, 72% and 32% at market weights 0, 0.2, 0.3 and 0.5, the score sandwich 86%, 84%, 79% and 59%, and the jackknife 92%, 93%, 91% and 74% (400 seeds): the 85 to 95% line is met up to 0.3 and not at 0.5, where the shape's sampling distribution is set by the few extreme market days of a 500-day sample. Tail diagnostics and laws: `hill` and `hillCurve` (xi over the root of k as its error), `meanExcess` and `meanExcessShape` (the weighted slope of the mean-excess line, xi over 1 - xi), `thresholdStability` (the pooled shape across a ladder of quantiles and the lowest from which every higher estimate lies within the spread two nested samples allow), `semiParametricLaw` (an empirical body and generalised Pareto tails fitted on each side, with `sf`, `cdf`, `isf` and `quantile`, the signed or the absolute law) and `covariateGpd` (a log scale linear in covariates with one shape, Newton in the coefficients inside Brent in the shape, standard errors from the step-scaled Hessian, `covariateGpdSf`), each held to numpy or scipy in `fixtures-moves-tail.json`. Reference scale and challengers: `referenceScale` (the root mean square of the sessions that are not scheduled events; counting an earnings session is the planted wrong build `sigma-ref-includes-earnings`, which the kill matrix now arms and kills), `dateJackknife` (a delete-a-block standard error for any statistic of the exceedance groups), and `hierarchicalShape` (a random-effects fit: each name's exact profile likelihood in the shape on a grid, a normal prior on the shapes with its centre and spread found by marginal likelihood, shrunken posterior means) with `CHALLENGERS`, the registry in which the pooled shape is the one baseline, the hierarchical fit a registered challenger and the per-name fit the reference; the suite scores all three, and the hierarchical fit has 17% less error than pooling when shapes spread by sector-like 0.15 and is no better when they are equal. The daily generator in `tests/truth/worlds.mjs` gains W1m (GJR-GARCH with Hansen skew-t innovations, sector correlation and a per-name log-scale error) and W2 (W1m with Poisson jumps, scheduled earnings sessions and splits, with adjusted and unadjusted closes). `FLOWS_EVT_DEEP=1` re-measures at 400 seeds; `node tests/flows-evt.mjs --write-bands` rewrites the committed bands.

### `shared/flows-stats.js`, `tests/gen-stats-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-stats.json`, `tests/flows-stats-contract.mjs`

The statistics leaf: no imports, no clock, no randomness beyond the seeded `mulberry32`. Mean, sample variance and deviation, median, quantiles, share at or below, average-rank percentile, Pearson and Spearman, the Wilson interval, effective n under clustering, the t critical value and the mean interval, the Newey-West standard error, a seeded stationary bootstrap and its interval, Brier, log score, the reliability table with the Murphy decomposition (reliability - resolution + uncertainty + within-bin variance - 2 x within-bin covariance, exact for binned forecasts), Benjamini-Hochberg. A null never becomes a zero: an empty or non-numeric input answers `null`. `gen-stats-fixtures.py` (scipy, numpy, statsmodels; the versions and the generator's SHA-256 are in the fixture) writes the committed JSON the contract reads without Python. Consumers: `shared/flows-vol.js`, `shared/flows-features.js`, `shared/flows-cross.js`, and the medians of `shared/flows-political.js`, `flows-premium.js`, `flows-dossier.js`, `flows-variation.js`, `flows-market.js` and `scripts/flows-quant-pipeline.mjs`.

### `shared/flows-conviction.js`, `tests/flows-conviction.mjs`, `tests/gen-conviction-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-conviction.json`, `tests/conviction-sim.mjs`

Does conviction order outcomes (P2-22): a pure leaf over `flows-record.js`'s forward-close rule that scores each archived board name as a hit when it beat the session's equal-weight board average in its board's direction, fits `logit P(hit) = a + b·cnv/100` with a session-clustered sandwich and Student t on G-1 degrees of freedom, reads terciles of `cnv` with Wilson intervals on the effective count, the clustered top-minus-bottom gap and its minimum detectable size (2.8 standard errors), the pooled Spearman with a leave-one-session-out jackknife, and breadth's own coefficient. `evaluateConviction` returns `keep` (slope interval above zero and rising terciles), `relabel` (the number is an "Agreement index") or `pending` (under 60 scored sessions and 600 rows at the ten-session horizon, or no fit), published as `record.conviction`. The contract holds the estimators to numpy and scipy on 500 committed reference rows (`gen-conviction-fixtures.py` writes the JSON) and to simulated boards with a known slope. The History page that displayed it is removed.

### `shared/flows-time.js`, `tests/flows-time.mjs`

The Türkiye-time oracle, a leaf over `flows-freshness.js` alone. TRT is fixed UTC+3 arithmetic labelled with the literal `TRT` (Türkiye has no daylight time; the test proves it equal to `Europe/Istanbul` at every half hour of 2026-2028). `instantOf` takes a finite positive number, a `Date` or an ISO string with a time and an explicit `Z` or offset and answers null for a date-only, zone-less or impossible value, so a reader prints the dash; `clock`, `stamp`, `day` and `when` print TRT; `axisMinutes(v, sessionDay)` is minutes since 00:00 TRT of the session day, so a chart axis stays strictly increasing across TRT midnight when the winter close is 00:00, and `tick(m)` prints it modulo a day (never 24:00). `boundaries(day, clock)` derives pre-market, open, close and after-hours end in TRT only from `sessionOpen`, `sessionClose`, `easternInstant` and `closeMinutes`: 16:30 to 23:00 in US summer time, 17:30 to 00:00 in standard time, early closes 20:00 and 21:00; `sessionSaid`, `phaseSaid` and `nextOpenSaid` are the wordings. No importer yet, no asset. `TRT` is also in the Worker's `ASK_NOT_TICKERS`, because it is a real ticker.

### `shared/flows-plan-facts.js`, `tests/flows-plan-facts.mjs`

Every platform cap per plan in one table, a leaf that imports nothing. `RESOURCES` is the meter list (vendor day and minute, D1, Worker, Durable Object, Workers AI, Analytics Engine, R2, Queues, Logs, GitHub), `FACTS` the other caps; each cell carries its source id (`SOURCES`: page, how it was read, date) and the Free cell is the downgrade column, the Paid cell the design column. `FLOWS_PLAN_WORKERS` is `free` or `paid` (unset: `paid`; a misspelt value: `free`), `FLOWS_UW_PLAN` is `unknown`, `advanced`, `startup`, `startup-annual` or `kafka` (unset or unrecognised: `unknown`, the minimum of each vendor row); `wrangler.toml` and the nightly's build step both record `advanced`, the API Advanced plan the owner's screenshot of the vendor's dashboard named on 2026-10-19 (source `uw.dashboard`: 100M daily requests included, kept as the day cap although the page also says unlimited requests per day; a two-year lookback, 730 days; the daily window resetting at 20:00 ET, the clock fact `uw.reset`; websockets included, quoted in the source because the table has no entitlement cells), and staging records none. The dashboard states no minute or concurrency line, so those Advanced cells stay null, and a null line that has an `ADVISORY` entry resolves to it (`capFrom` `advisory`, `capSource` naming where the cap came from): the minute line every governor and budget reads on Advanced is the 500 working line, never unlimited. `vendorDay(env)` is the stated reset with its source and date, or `assumed` for a plan whose window nobody has read. `lines(env)` is the one view a governor or a budget test reads; no other file reads either variable. `renewalState` reports the Workers Paid period and does not change a line.

### `shared/flows-live.js`, `shared/flows-live-worker.js`

The live layer's key registry and pure builders; the Worker's Tier 1 tick, dispatch, watchdog, live ingest, `/api/flows/lk`, `/now`, `/tape` and read-time overlays. A strip row ends in `qa`, the vendor's `quote_time` as seconds behind the read, and the key's `ahead { n, maxS }` counts the stamps that run later than the read; `priorCloseBase` and `shapeStrips` fill a null `prev_close` from the last dated nightly close and say so in `prevFill`, and hold out a row the vendor dates before the session as an all-null row (DEPLOY.md 10.5i). `live:alerts:head` is the compact head of the same alerts record (the 20 largest-premium windows as facts, at most 8 KiB, breadth class, Actions writer): `shapeAlertsHead` builds it from the merged `live:alerts` payload the pass has just written, stamps it with that record's own `readAt` and vendor cursor, makes no vendor call, and `live.mjs` writes it only when `live:alerts` itself was written; it is the input compact enough for a model job to read without parsing the 120 KiB record.

### `shared/flows-basis.js`, `tests/flows-basis-contract.mjs`

The premium desk's basis: the vendor's `{data:{...}}` stock-state envelope, a spot that is only a regular-session price (`printOf`: the live print in the regular session, else the newest regular close), and the coherence gate between that spot and the chain (`coherence`: impossible asks, the strike bracket the `maybe_otm_only` request guarantees, one underlying fitted to the nearest expiries, a rebase past 0.25% or a refusal when the fit is too thin). The contract runs the Worker route in Node against a stubbed vendor. The desk's `asOf` is the New York date of the READ and `days` count from it; `UW_NOW` (an ISO instant, honoured only while `UW_BASE` redirects the vendor away from production) pins that read clock so the workerd suites' dated fixtures stay valid.

### `shared/flows-vendor-core.js`, `tests/flows-rt-contract.mjs`

The pure leaf every vendor client reads: `vendorBaseInfo` and `vendorBase` accept `UW_BASE` only as the production origin, `http(s)` loopback (`127.0.0.1`, `localhost`) or an `http(s)` `*.test` host with no credentials, path, query or fragment, and otherwise answer the production origin with status `invalid`; `vendorRedirected` is true only for a loopback or `.test` base and is what unlocks the test-only `UW_NOW` and `FLOWS_RT_SCALE`; `vendorUrl`, `unwrap` (the vendor's `{data}` envelope), `classifyStatus` (`http_429`, `http_5xx`, `http_4xx`) and `retryAfterMs`. `uwFetch` in `worker.js` and the rail's REST adapter use it; the pipeline and the legs still read `FLOWS_UW_BASE_URL` directly. The allowed and refused base table is in `flows-rt-contract`, the Worker half in `flows-basis-contract`.

### `shared/flows-vendor-ops.js`

The vendor operation registry, a data-only leaf: one entry for each of the 79 operations production reads, with `id`, `path` (the call template; `op` is the spec's operation when the two differ), `plan`, `licence`, `envelope`, `klass` (a `PACKET_CLASSES` name), `limiter` (`ondemand` for an operation the Worker serves on demand, `null` for one only an engine reads), `liveOnly`, `maxBytes` and `reads` (the fields the code reads, which the weekly probe holds strict); `opById`, `pathFor(id, params)` (each parameter is encoded as one path segment and a missing one is refused), `opsFor(limiter)`, `BUDGETS` (the consumer ceilings and windows) and `PLAN` (the observed key and the Startup plan). No vendor path is spelled anywhere else in `worker.js`, `shared/` or `scripts/` outside the fakes, `scripts/flows-probe.mjs` derives its strict read list from here, and `tests/flows-probe-contract.mjs` scans for a stray literal, holds every entry to the committed spec or `liveOnly`, and holds each `BUDGETS` figure equal to the code constant it names.

### `shared/flows-tape-stats.js`, `tests/flows-tape-stats.mjs`

The option-print classifier, a leaf that imports only `flows-display-round.js` (the whole-percent rule) and reads no clock: `tierOf` sorts a fill against the NBBO into A (at or above the ask), a, m (the midpoint), b, B (at or below the bid) or n (no usable quote); `sideTagOf` reads the vendor's one side tag and `classifyPrint` returns the row (`side` -1, 0 or +1, `tier`, `tag`, `parity`, `basis`, a `flags` bitmask, `signed`). Where the tag and the tier disagree the print is unsigned and counted, never guessed. `sweepOf` reads both encodings of a sweep, `report_flags` containing `intermarket_sweep` or an upstream code (`upstream_condition_detail` on REST, `trade_code` on the socket) containing the whole token `isoi`, and never the equity enum. Multi-leg codes, canceled prints and the `dividend` and `arbitrage` tags keep a print out of every signed total; a large print exists only as ten times a premium floor the caller states. `aggregate` sums asked, bid and unclassified premium over rolling windows and `reconciles` holds them to gross within 1e-9; `askSplit`, `askShare` and `askShareRead` are the one ask-share definition, the rule of `tests/fixtures-ask-share.json` (a side counts only as a non-negative number, a window needs a positive premium, sides past the premium are scaled to it, windows are pooled, the coverage is sided dollars over every positive premium, and 0.65, 0.35 and 0.5 are compared as the whole percents printed). `tests/flows-tape-stats.mjs` runs synthetic tapes only, because the repository is public, and reads `tests/fixtures-ask-share.json` when that table is in the tree.

### `shared/flows-premium.js`, `tests/flows-premium-contract.mjs`

The premium desk's ranking: gates, annualization, the strike divisor and `rankChain`. Its mid implied volatility inverts against a per-expiry forward (`forwardPlan`): the chain's own put-call parity over up to ten near-the-money two-sided pairs (mid of at least 0.05, spread under half the mid) when there are three or more, else the card's implied carry (`deskCarry`: grade 2 or better, and only when `coherence` returned `ok`), else the rate alone. A parity forward or a carry whose log gap from the rate forward exceeds `forwardGapLimit(years)` (2% plus 0.5 a year, capped at 15%) is not believed. A rebased chain takes no carry because its fitted spot has already absorbed the carry over the fit horizon, the nearest expiries, only: later expiries of a rebased chain keep the rate-only forward unless parity places them, so their mid IV stays biased (about 1.1 vol points at 180 days and 2.1 at 360 days at a 3% carry on an out-of-the-money-only chain), a limit pinned in `tests/flows-basis-contract.mjs`. `rankChain` returns the choice per expiry as `forwards` (an empty list on a `mismatch` payload). `premiumLine` is the one rule for Premium ÷ collateral (`premiumC`, `collateralC` and `premiumRatio` on every priced row, `premiumRatio` among `RANK_KEYS`), and `roundCents` is the one half-up rounding every cents figure here uses. It also holds the short-call oracle the Short calls view is held to: `SC_DEFAULTS`, `roundCents` (integer cents, half-up), `parseContracts`, `parsePercent`, `shortCallCollateral` (delta-adjusted, Reg-T style, shares held), `dividendsToExpiry`, `shortCallLine` and `planShortCalls`. Sizing carries time value beside cash: `sizeToBuyingPower` returns `collectible` (the cash in), `timeValue` and `intrinsicValue` in integer cents, and `yieldOnDeployed` is time value over what is deployed, so an in-the-money line no longer counts its in-the-money value as a return; `betterDeployment` is the one comparator (most time value, then the smaller deployment) that `planBuyingPower` and `planShortCalls` pick the best line by, and a line with no time value is never best. `priceSale` snaps a difference under a nano-dollar to zero, so a bid equal to the intrinsic value has exactly zero time value. `crossesEarnings(expiry, date, announce, { asOf, readMs })` answers `null` when the report precedes every possible sale (`reportPrecedesSale`, with `saleDayOf` rolling to the next session after the close, DST and early closes included) and `rankChain` names each expiry's forward figure `F` beside its method.

### `shared/flows-quant-dealer.js`

The dealer gamma profile (`gammaProfile`): a leaf with no imports. Per contract it precomputes `1/(sigma sqrt(T))`, the affine part of d1 in ln x and the signed weight, so each grid point is one logarithm and one exponential per contract instead of a full set of Black-Scholes greeks; output equals the greeks-based profile to 1e-12 of the profile's peak and the flip to 1e-9, at about a tenth of the CPU (`tests/flows-quant.mjs` holds the equality against a reference copy and the CPU ratio through `tests/lib/cpu-budget.mjs`).

### `shared/flows-fact-ids.js`, `tests/flows-fact-ids.mjs`, `tests/fixtures-fact-ids.json`

The engine fact-id registry: one frozen entry per id `engineFacts` emits (`{meaning, unit, tenor, gradeRule, claimLines?, since, retired?}`), the `move.mad.<expiry>` family by prefix, `factEntry`, and the claim-line table `flows-neuron.js` re-exports as `CLAIM_LINES` (the `iv.*` ids use 0.25/0.75, `skew.rr25.30.pct` 0.2/0.8). A fact id never changes meaning: a new meaning is a new id and the old one is retired. `iv.pct.30`, the rank once stored under a percentile-looking id, is retired (`retired: {since, by}`, `retiredIds()`): `engineFacts` no longer emits it, it has no claim line and no label, and the dossier drops it from a card stored before the retirement (`RETIRED_ENGINE_FACTS`). The test holds every emitted id registered, every registered id emitted, every read of an engine id by the engine, the structures, the card, the Neuron and the reading (`ENGINE_LABELS`, `options.engine.<id>`) on a live entry, and the registry equal to the committed snapshot, which is append-only by review: a changed meaning, unit, tenor or claim line fails it with the instruction to retire the id. The dossier's packet ids outside `options.engine.*` are not registered.

The per-name dossier, a leaf over `flows-cross.js`, `flows-stats.js`, `flows-vendor-ops.js` and `flows-tape-stats.js` (the flow packet's ask share is that leaf's one rule: the ask dollars over the premium the vendor sided, stated only when at least half the premium is sided, beside the sided share): the packet model, one pure builder per kind (twelve), the sanitiser every third-party string passes, `renderDossierForModel`, `dossierFacts`, `dossierSilences`, `dossierFingerprint`. ### `shared/flows-dossier-vendor.js`

The vendor side of the dossier: `unwrap` of the `{data}` envelope, one reducer per route (13) that returns a small extract and names its fields (`DOSSIER_READS`, the list `scripts/flows-probe-list.json` holds strict; a spec name that `WIRE_RENAMES` maps to its wire name is read only as a fallback, the strict list holds the wire name instead, and the entry is retired when the spec is refreshed), and the dossier's quote extract. ### `shared/flows-ai-salvage.js`, `tests/flows-ai-salvage.mjs`

The salvage leaf for a model reply that was cut off. `parseReadingPartial(text)` is a single-pass scanner (string, escape and depth state, no recursion, no eval) that returns every array element and top-level member that closed before the cut, marked `truncated`; `salvageReading(text, dossier, tags, opts)` vets that exactly as `vetReading` vets a whole reply, discards the model's own `unknown` list and puts `unknownEntries(dossier)` in its place with the note that the list of what is not known is the site's own, still needs `now` and a second section, and names `read:salvaged:<kept>` as its guard. `opts.trim` (off by default) also keeps the element cut inside its cites, cut back at a sentence boundary to the sentences the closed cites support and carrying only the cites a surviving sentence uses. It imports `flows-reading.js` and `flows-ask.js`, nothing imports it yet (the queue consumer and the output budget are F-45), and it makes no model call. The suite cuts nine valid replies at every UTF-8 byte offset and checks the items against the whole reply, the growth of the set, the sentence-boundary trims and the unknown list, and plants six wrong builds.

### `shared/flows-targets.js`, `tests/flows-targets.mjs`

The canonical large-move targets, a leaf with no imports: the frozen definition table (35 ids: `move.k{2,3,4}.h{1,5}` with `.up` and `.dn`, `move.im{1,1.5,2}.h1`, `move.x{5,10,20}.h{1,5}`, `move.earn.im`, `move.earn.ratio`, `move.ret.h{1,5,21}` at the fifteen-quantile union, `move.live.res` as a reading, `move.z63.h{1,5}` as a sensitivity), its `TARGETS_VERSION` and `targetsHash` (a definition changed without a version bump fails the test's pinned history), `sigmaRef` (1.4826 x MAD of the trailing 252 regular-session, split-adjusted, break-free, non-event log returns, at least 200 else the name is not forecast and counted with the reason, `clockVersion` `sess-v0` with the weekend variance 0; a later estimate is a new id for new issues only), `impactSession(report_date, report_time)` (defined once, here), `threshold` and `resolves` (every event row carries a scale and a finite k, `move.earn.im` on `sigmaQE` of the impact session; a missing scale is `{ok:false, reason:"no scale"}` and resolves to null; the vendor's `implied_move_perc` is refused as a source), `earnRatio` within one track, `rarityText` (a k-multiple is always printed with how often such days occur for that name, the empirical route floored at 1 in 250, the model route at the audited `pFloor` it is given, the scan p printed `p below 0.00002` and never as 1 in N) and `cardField` (about 30 bytes a card). 670 names take about 40 ms of thread CPU.

### `shared/flows-moves0.js`, `tests/flows-moves0.mjs`

The IV-days lens on Market's Extremes (Risers and Fallers, `Raw change` and `In IV-days`, on for the owner's account and off for members, the worker passing `rtIsOwner` to `marketPage` as `data-lens`) and the IV-days column in Home's Leaders. `m = chg / (iv30p / sqrt(252))`, with `iv30p` the previous trading day's 30-day IV (the vendor's `iv30d_1d`, never a current IV, which rises with the move and would rank the largest shocks lowest) and the vendor's `implied_move_perc` never a unit; a name with `iv30p` null or not positive is on no list, never zero; a name with earnings inside 21 sessions is ranked apart; the median and the count beyond 3 are over the clean names; one line of copy (`A ranking, not a test: no error rate measured.`) and the caption `IV as of the previous close.`, none of the words unusual, alert, screen or rare. The leaf is the oracle and the pages carry the one division, since a page cannot import a module; the test holds both pages to its digits. Market ranks one population at a time and names it: the whole nightly universe once the nightly for the live rows' session has landed, the live roster alone while the live session is newer, the nightly when the live rows are older or unavailable. Home reads `/api/flows/universe?cols=t,sec,chg,iv30p,ed` (14.5 KB of JSON for 670 names, about 3.3 KB gzipped) for the event mark and the nightly fallback. The order is held while a mouse pointer is on the module. Chromium against `page.route` stubs, no server.

### `shared/flows-cross.js`

The `universe` payload's column store (`UNIVERSE_COLUMNS`, one integer column per key, decoded by `units`) and its 100 KiB budget, shed by column priority when over. The nightly's dealer columns are dollars per 1% move over average daily dollar volume (`gexAdv`, `dex`, `vanna`, `charm`) and keep the vendor's sign; `im5` and `im30` are the vendor's implied-move fractions. On 670 synthetic names the payload is 96,158 bytes without them, 108,249 with them unbudgeted, and 100,505 after the shedder drops `adx`, `dGamma` and `gexRatio`; `iv30p` (the vendor's `iv30d_1d`, the previous trading day's 30-day implied volatility, the denominator of the IV-days ranking) adds 2,681 bytes, so the same 670 names are 111,139 unbudgeted and 100,609 after the shedder drops `adx`, `dGamma`, `gexRatio` and `dIv1m` (priority 5, no reader). `iv30p` is priority 1 and is never shed, and `charm` is priority 3 so the Neuron screen keeps it; `tests/flows-legs-contract.mjs` holds both on the 670-name fixture. The run's `shed` list is the measurement that counts. `edUnstated` (a name to `ed + 1` for the reports of unstated time within `LIVE_ELAPSED_MAX` (2) sessions of the session, nearest first; when more than `ED_UNSTATED_MAX` (200) qualify, the group of equal `ed` that the cap falls in is dropped whole, so the kept map is complete for every nearer group, with the full count in `edUnstatedN`) is the candidate the live pages need to age a forward report whose time is not stated, and `edUnstatedThrough` is the greatest `ed` for which a name absent from the map has a stated time (2 when nothing was cut, else the dropped group's `ed` less one; a payload without it is read as knowing nothing, so every unlisted report is taken as unknown-time and never as stated); a projection that asks for `ed` carries both. `projectionColumns` and `projectUniverse` serve `GET /api/flows/universe?cols=t,sec,chg,iv30p,ed` (one to twelve column names, normalised and sorted, a 400 `invalid_cols` otherwise): the asked columns of the one payload row, about 14.5 KB for 670 names and five columns, kept in the Cache API for 300 s so a repeat read costs 0 D1 rows, never written over the whole universe's last good copy.

### `shared/flows-activity.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/activity.mjs`

The Unusual page's dated exchange-code unit. After the chain pass the nightly makes one call, `/api/option-activity/unusual` with `date` the session, `limit` 200 (the vendor's page maximum) and the largest premium first; `CALL_COST.activity` counts it, and `FLOWS_UNUSUAL_V2=off` makes no call and publishes `activity.status: "off"`. `buildActivityRows` keeps a row whose option symbol parses, whose volume is positive and whose last fill falls on the session's Eastern day (another day is counted in `offDate`; no fill time is kept and counted in `undated`); a sweep, floor, multi-leg, stock multi-leg or cross volume (`ACTIVITY_CLASSES` order) that is negative or above the row's volume is withheld as null for that class only. The classes overlap and are never summed. `mergeActivity` puts `cls` and `pm` (executed premium) on the ranked contract rows that are also in the screen, joined by the pipeline's own contract id; an unmatched row carries neither key. The payload gains `activity` (status, `asOf`, counts, at most 30 rows and 3 a name) and `basis.activity`; `volumeAsOf` stays null because the chain counter is still undated. The page adds the row description and a list of the screen's largest by premium. The lexicon test names "sweep" as an exception for `basis.activity` alone.

### `shared/flows-dispersion.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/dispersion.mjs`, `tests/flows-dispersion.mjs`

Correlation and dispersion context for the Nasdaq-100. The leaf (imports only `flows-stats.js`) turns QQQ's holdings weights, the index's and members' 30-day implied volatilities and daily closes into the implied correlation (the weighted average pairwise correlation that reproduces the index's implied variance), the realised one over 21 and 63 sessions (the same quantity on the members' return series, equal to the brute-force weighted average of pairwise correlations), their difference and the index-to-members volatility ratio, each with its coverage: a member reporting inside the 30 days is excluded from the implied side and counted, a member with a gap or no movement in the window from the realised side. The leg reads members' bars from the run's candles and fetches closes for the rest at most 100 a week, caching them in the row's `state` (the Worker drops it); a new ISO week starts a new budget.

### `shared/flows-ready.js`, `tests/flows-ready.mjs`

The readiness leaf, a module over `flows-time.js` and `flows-freshness.js` alone. `readiness(state, now)` answers for the current session whether Flows can serve the open: the nightly landed for `expectedNightlySession` (the run record `meta`, then `universe`, `regime`, `events` and both `board` sides each at or beyond it), Tier 1 ticking in the session (`tier1At` within 11 minutes, `tier1OkAt` within 16, a holiday verdict skipped, an 11-minute grace after the open), the rail when the caller is an owner and the day is a trading day inside 04:00-20:00 ET (off, unreachable, degraded or a roster error each block), and the clock row. It returns `ok`, `blockers` (`code` and a plain `sentence`), `checks` (`ok`, `blocked` or `skipped` each), `expected`, the session the open belongs to, `minutesToOpen` and `opens` in TRT through `flows-time.js`. `healthOf` is the three-field public body. A null or unreadable input is a blocker, never a pass. `tests/flows-ready.mjs` holds each blocker from both sides at a pinned clock, the pre-market news cadence and its kill switch, and the one-call-one-row write.

### `server/routes/flows-ready.js`

The ready family in the route table: `flowsReadyRows(deps)` is `GET /api/flows/ready` behind the Flows session and the anonymous `GET /api/health`. Readiness reads the clock row and one `store.metadata` call for the six nightly keys (about six rows and two trips, never a payload), asks the rail (`railReadiness` in `shared/flows-rt-routes.js`, a 1.5 s read of the object's own status) only for an owner, and answers `shared/flows-ready.js`'s body with `owner`. `FLOWS_READY` and `FLOWS_HEALTH` in `wrangler.toml` are `on`; anything else is a 404 `ready_off` or `health_off`. Health is `{ok, asOf, session}` computed from the calendar, no store, no clock row, no rail. The pre-market news pass (`premarketNewsTick` in `shared/flows-live-worker.js`, `FLOWS_PREMARKET_NEWS`) runs on the half-hourly housekeeping firing between 04:00 and 09:30 ET on trading days, makes one vendor call (the news headlines, limit 100) and upserts `live:news` for the day's session, at most 11 calls and 11 rows a day (`BUDGETS.premarketNews`).

### `server/routes/flows-dispatch.js`, `tests/flows-dispatch-check.mjs`

The owner-only dispatch credential check: `flowsDispatchRows(deps)` is `POST /api/flows/dispatch-check` behind the Flows session. A non-owner is `403 owner_only` (`FLOWS_RT_USERS` through `isOwner`), a cross-origin call `403`, and a second call inside 60 s in one isolate `429 rate_limited` with `Retry-After: 60` (`state.dispatchCheckAt`). `checkDispatchCredential` in `shared/flows-live-worker.js` makes one read-only `GET /repos/{repo}/actions/workflows/{FLOWS_LIVE_WORKFLOW}` with the Worker's token (200 `ok`, 401 `bad-token`, 403 and 404 `no-access`, a network failure `unreachable`) and one `dispatchWorkflow` of the live workflow with `{origin: manual, drill: true}`, which opens one witness issue and closes it at once under the concurrency group `flows-live-drill`. The outcome is written to `flows_clock.dispatch_why` through `recordDispatchWhy` (one upsert, failure ignored, nothing written for a missing token). The body is `{ok, read, dispatch, recorded, repo, workflow, at}` and never carries the token or a header value. `tests/flows-dispatch-check.mjs` drives `worker.js` with a scripted GitHub for seven status pairs, the token-absent and store-failure cases, every principal and the rate limit, and fails seven planted wrong builds of the route.

### `shared/flows-fixture-names.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/taint.mjs`, `tests/fixture-guard.mjs`, `tests/flows-fixture-guard-render.mjs`

The fixture guard (SYN-01 to SYN-11 of the 2026-10-13 synthetic audit): nothing invented can reach a member. `shared/flows-fixture-names.js` is the reserved namespace, a leaf that imports nothing: `FIXTURE_TICKER_RE` (SY, one letter, three digits, or GONE and one digit, which no exchange symbol can match because symbols hold no digits), `FIXTURE_TOKEN_RE` (a whole JSON string, never a word inside a headline), `isFixtureTicker`, `fixtureNameIn`, `fixtureMarked` (a top-level `fixture: true`), `fixtureHost` (loopback or a `.test` name, the only waiver), `fixtureRefusal(key, text)` and `FIXTURE_REFUSALS` (the 422 `fixture_ticker` and `fixture_payload` rows `server/errors.js` repeats). Worker door: `server/routes/flows-ingest.js` `contentRefusal(url, key, text)` refuses a fixture ticker key on every method, a body naming one and a stamped body before any D1 statement, and passes the request host into `ingestLive` for live keys; it is the one seam a later door (schema, compare-and-set, quarantine) extends. The read side answers 400 `invalid_ticker` for a fixture name off a test host (`requireTicker`, the summary route, the rail's `f`). No variable can switch the guard off: only the request host waives it. Pipeline: `publish()` in `scripts/flows-nightly/store.mjs` refuses before the network (`publishRefusal`: a fixture name, a stamped body, or `fixture_code` when `scripts/flows-legs/taint.mjs` says a fake ran) unless the ingest host is a test host, sets the exit code and throws an error with `fixtureLeak`, which `createStageRunner.run` rethrows even for an isolated stage; every dry-run payload that is an object is written with `fixture: true`; `targetRefusal` stops a run whose `FLOWS_UW_BASE_URL` is a redirect or invalid base from publishing to a non-test ingest, before the first vendor call, nightly and live. `taint.mjs` is a leaf (`touch`, `tainted`, `clearTaint`, the last for tests only); every export of `fixtures.mjs` and each fake constructor touches it on entry. Backstops: the health gate adds a `fixture-name` failure when any live body it already reads names a fixture; the client (`FlowsUI.fixture` in `assets/js/flows-ui.js`, a copy of the token and host rules the suite holds equal to the leaf) quarantines a page that is sent a fixture name or `fixture: true` (hidden main, the unavailable pill, one console error, later Flows requests held), drops rail frames that carry one and counts them in `stats.fixture`, and refuses a fixture `?t=` before asking for a card; `scripts/render-preview.mjs` runs on a `.test` origin and prints a Fixture data ribbon on every page. `tests/fixture-guard.mjs` (class N) holds the namespace table with five wrong rules, the Worker door and the pipeline battery against copies of the source with planted wrong builds each of which must fail, the dry-run stamp, the stage rethrow, the configuration preflight as spawned processes, the health line, the static confinement scan (a fixture token only in tests, docs, the fakes, the preview fixtures and the leaf; a fake called outside a dry-run branch; every fake taints on entry; only tests clear the taint) with its own mutations, the Vision sample-world strings absent from served files, and the dry emit stamped. `tests/flows-fixture-guard-render.mjs` (class C, `page.route`, no server) renders the long board and ticker from the dry emit on the production origin and on `.test` and loopback origins, the rail on both, a stamped real-named card, and five planted wrong builds of the served scripts.

### `shared/flows-call-meter.js`

The vendor call meter, a pure leaf over the operation registry. `opIdOf(path)` names the registered operation a path belongs to (a literal route wins over a template that would also fit it, so `/api/darkpool/recent` is `darkpool-recent` and not `darkpool`; anything unregistered is `other`), `classOf(status, kind)` files an attempt as 2xx, 3xx, 4xx, 429, 5xx, timeout or network, `codeOf` names a failed one exactly (the HTTP status, or `timeout`, `network` or `parse`, an unreadable body being in the network class), and `createCallMeter` records one line per attempt (operation, milliseconds, declared bytes, class) into per-operation totals, a class tally and fixed latency buckets, folding operations past its cap into `other`; a failed attempt also counts under its code (at most eight codes an operation) and the first three keep a sample `{ code, ms, t, pg, a }`: the ticker's shape (`shapeOf`: plain, list, dot, slash, digit, long, other, read by `tickerOf` from the path slot or the query), the page (`pageOf`: the offset, else the page number) and the attempt, never the ticker, a URL or a token. `quantileMs`, `summarize` (the slowest operations by the time they cost, and `fails`: every operation with a failed attempt, worst first, with its codes and samples, cut by `fitFails` to twelve operations and 2,048 bytes with the cut counted in `failsOmitted`), `wireDelta`, `fails()` (the run's failures by operation and code) and `failsDelta` with `failLine` read it. The nightly's `uw()` records every attempt into one (`wire` in `scripts/flows-nightly/vendor.mjs`); `createStageRunner` takes a `wire()` reading and puts `{ ms, kb, bad }` on the record of a stage that made calls, and none on a stage that made none, and given a `fails()` reading adds `fail`, the stage's own failures as `op code: n` worst first (`multi-leg 422: 84`), clipped at 120 characters, so a dry run adds nothing and the golden hashes hold; `meta.vendor` is the summary and exists only when the run called the vendor. The Worker's `uwFetch` records into one held in its single state object when `FLOWS_METER` is `on`, and the same switch adds `Server-Timing: total;dur=<ms>` to every `/api` response; off, `uwFetch` is the unmetered function and no response changes.

### `shared/flows-products.js`, `tests/flows-registry-contract.mjs`, `tests/lib/emit-keys.mjs`

The product registry, a data-only leaf over the live key registry: one frozen entry for each thing the pipeline stores (the 24 nightly globals, the four dated archive families with their write-once and permanent flags, `card`, `card-x` and `hist` keyed by ticker, and the live keys with the writer, class and byte cap the live registry gives them), each with its key or pattern, level, executor, epoch, byte cap, volatile stamps and the minutes after the close it is due. It also holds the six states a product can be in (`STATE`: ok, quiet, failed, unfetched, stale, pending), the twelve panel products a name's page shows (`PANELS`) and the contract each tier is held to (`CONTRACT`: a screen name is held to a quote, a chart, levels, a move, a volatility and a reading; a board or focus name to twelve), and which of the three name keys each tier should hold. `tests/flows-registry-contract.mjs` holds it to the door's grammar in `server/store.js` in both directions (every exact key the door accepts is a registered global and the three grouped boards are the rest), to the dated-archive and permanent-archive rules, to the ingest and ledger list kinds, to readiness's keys, to the live registry and the health gate's live keys, and to the dry-run emit, which must write nothing unregistered and every registered global. `tests/lib/emit-keys.mjs` maps the emit's files back to keys.

### `shared/flows-contracts.js`, `tests/flows-contracts.mjs`

The payload contracts, a pure leaf over the registry and the card schema version. `validate(key, payload, ctx)` answers `{ ok, structural, flags, baseOk, tier }`: a structural failure (not an object, a key no product owns, a ticker that is not its key's, a session that is not a real date, a stamp that is not an instant, a schema version outside 2 to the builder's, a `depth` or panel state outside the vocabulary, a missing core panel, a universe whose columns do not match its count, a roster depth outside the five, a board on the wrong side or with a row that has no ticker, a number that is not finite, a dated key filed under another day, or a `SYN###` name or `fixture: true` anywhere outside a test) refuses the payload, while a soft flag (a panel an older card never had, a price of zero, a one-day move past minus 95%, a ticker outside the universe) never does. `checkPayload` returns the first failure, `canonical` and `canonicalHash` form the order-free, stamp-free content hash a door would compare. Nothing calls it from the Worker or the nightly yet. `tests/flows-contracts.mjs` validates every payload the dry-run emit writes, shows that without the test allowance every one that names a synthetic ticker is refused for that alone, and refuses an invalid corpus of planted payloads each by its code and path.

### `shared/flows-coverage.js`, `server/routes/flows-pipeline.js`, `tests/flows-coverage.mjs`

What the pipeline can say about each name. `shared/flows-coverage.js` is pure: `cardProducts(card, cardX, hist)` reads a card into a state for each of the twelve panel products (a read that failed, a panel never requested and a panel that read and found nothing are different codes, and a chart of fewer than 200 bars or a surface of fewer than three expiries is quiet), `screenProducts` does the same from a universe row for a name with no card, `coverageLedger` is the whole-universe ledger (a column of codes for every product, counts, the contract's misses by product, the 100 worst), and `verifyCoverage` is the key-level check: from ids and sessions alone it holds every universe name to the name keys its roster tier should have for the expected session, with the predicates P1 (the card is the expected session's), P2 (card-x and hist carry the card's session), P4 (the roster dates the card as the card is dated) and P7 (the universe row has sig, f2, f3 and iv30p), a conservation check that every name is in exactly one state of every product, and a `complete` that is false for any failed or stale key, any stale or missing global, a cut or unreadable listing or no expected session. `GET /api/flows/coverage` (`server/routes/flows-pipeline.js`, `flowsPipelineRows`) serves that report to an owner and no one else: `FLOWS_COVERAGE` is `on` in `wrangler.toml` and anything but `on` is a 404 `coverage_off`, a member who is not an owner a 403, an unreadable universe a 503. It reads the universe and roster rows, the metadata (ids, sessions) of the registered globals and one listing of the three name kinds, about 500 rows in production and at most 2,027 (`COVERAGE_ROW_CEILING`), and holds the answer 60 s in the Cache API, behind the owner check. With `FLOWS_METER` on it adds the Worker's vendor call summary. `tests/flows-coverage.mjs` works every state on the dry-run emit and on planted holes, and drives the route through the router with a fake store and cache.

### `scripts/flows-legs/sigma-ledger.mjs`, `tests/flows-data-fixes.mjs`

The typical-day scale for the whole universe. The nightly holds daily bars only for carded names, so `runSigmaLedger` keeps a rotating ledger inside the `regime` payload (`sigmaLedger`: ticker, scale at 1e5, count, `f2`, `f3`, day, plus a retry list for names short of history): tonight's fresh scales win, then up to 240 names with no scale or the oldest scale are re-read from the vendor's one-year daily bars, an entry older than 45 days is dropped, and a name short of 200 closes is not retried for 20 days. The universe publishes `sig`, `f2` and `f3` from the merged map with `sigmaBasis` (covered, fresh, rotated, short, oldest day), so coverage reaches an 856-name universe in about four nights at 240 vendor calls a night, never a guess. `shared/flows-events.js` `fitEvents` sheds far-dated rows of the `events` payload to stay under 120 KiB, below the 128 KiB ingest cap whose 413 stopped the events key advancing on 2026-10-15 and 2026-10-16. The suite uses synthetic bars only.

### `server/routes/flows-ops.js`, `server/ops.js`, `tests/server-ops.mjs`

The owner operations family in the route table: `flowsOpsRows(deps)` is one `GET /api/flows/ops` row behind the Flows session, then `FLOWS_OPS` (`wrangler.toml`, `on`; anything else is a 404 `ops_off`, read before the owner check) and then the owner check (a member who is not an owner is a 403 `forbidden`, a POST is the 405 before the session). `server/ops.js` is the I/O: one D1 batch of the last seven `flows_ledger` days, the `flows_clock` row, the eleven live keys by primary key with `json_extract` of `fresh.vendorAt` guarded by `json_valid` (never `json_each`), the model day's `flows_ai_usage` and `flows_ai_usage_model` rows, and with `?part=ai` the two outcome statements `readAiBlock` sends (`foldCounters`), and with `?part=coverage` one primary-key lookup of the fixed `COVERAGE_KEYS` (the nightly and shared keys, never a card row): `length(payload)`, the write time and the session date of each, shaped by `coverageView` against the 100 KiB `UNIVERSE_BUDGET_BYTES` (a key never written is `present: false`, not zero bytes); at most 24 rows by default, 24 plus the key count with coverage and 280 with the outcome block, 0 written. The body (`v: 1`) carries the ledger days, the clock with Tier 1's verdict and the GitHub dispatch refusal, each stored feed's age and distance behind the vendor's stamp with its live and stale lines from the freshness table, the vendor reading (the newest of the clock row's and the Worker's own, from `x-uw-daily-req-count`) with the vendor window it was read in and whether that is the current one (`vendorWindow`: from 20:00 ET to 20:00 ET as `vendorDay` states it, a reading before 20:00 ET in the window that ends that evening and one after it in the next; the UTC day for a plan whose reset is unread), the current window with its basis, source and date, `dayBoundary` `stated` or `assumed`, and the engines' own call counts, the model day's spend against the enforced cap (`aiCapOf`: 30,000 neurons, 2,500 calls, readings stop at 75%, not the 10,000 free allowance), `lines(env)`, `planOf(env)` and `renewalState`, the enforced limits (`limitsOf`, held equal to `wrangler.toml` by the suite and by `flows-vendor-budget`) and an allowlist of switch values (`OPS_SWITCHES`, no secret-shaped name). A failed batch is a 200 with every stored part null and `why: store`. No vendor call, no model call, no plan variable read outside `shared/flows-plan-facts.js`. The suite runs the family over a seeded `node:sqlite` fake: gates and their order, one trip, the row ceilings, a refused batch, a payload that is not JSON, plan and cap variables, the switch allowlist and the Worker scans.

### `shared/flows-deepen.js`, `scripts/flows-nightly/sections/deepen.mjs`, `tests/flows-deepen.mjs`, `docs/deepening.md`

The deepening rotation (design in `docs/deepening.md`): every name in the universe gets a full deep card within a bounded number of nights, without anyone choosing tickers. `shared/flows-deepen.js` is a pure leaf over `flows-universe.js`' session counter: `governor` sizes a night as the least of the cap (470, `FLOWS_DEEPEN_CAP`, hard ceiling 900), the backlog, the calls the day line leaves (85 per cent of `lines(env)['uw.day']`, which is the plan's 100M on the recorded Advanced plan, so this term no longer binds, and the 60,000 working line for an unread plan, less the larger of what the run has spent and the key's own daily count, `stats.dailySeen`, read from the vendor's counter header, and the baseline with its margin, at 39 calls a name) and the time the deadline leaves (80 per cent of the 90 minute `DEADLINE_MS`, at 6.65 s a name), and returns the pace that keeps the rotation under 80 per cent of the minute line (the minute line alone sets it, so a raised day line raises no rate); `planNight` reads the ledger (the `deepen` key: `at[T]` the session of a name's last deep read, `tries[T]` consecutive failures with a 1, 2, 4, 8 session backoff), takes never-read names first by liquidity and then the oldest read, and returns the names that are carried (a deep read within 40 sessions whose stored card is the one the ledger dated) so that no shallower card, cross read or card-x replaces them; `settleNight` counts a name read only when its card and card-x landed; an unreadable ledger chooses nothing and protects every card. `scripts/flows-nightly/sections/deepen.mjs` is the I/O: `deepen-plan` (isolated, after the boards) enriches the chosen names the run had not and adds them to the deep build that the existing chain, per-name read, volatility, flow, ownership, earnings, engine and card-x stages already run, and `deepen-ledger` (isolated, before the roster) writes the ledger. A rotation card is published with `depth: "deepened"` (roster tier `deep`, held to the board's contract) and its own session date; rotation names are kept out of the ideas list and its track record, the board's chain columns, the unusual screen, the vanna probe and the risk-reversal cohort, and `FLOWS_DEEPEN=off` returns the nightly to what it was. Borrow, short volume and the earnings history are now read for every carded name, which is why a cross-section name's Positioning and Earnings were empty. `tests/flows-deepen.mjs` holds the governor, the pick order, the ledger and the hold rules to worked answers, runs the 856-name universe through `tests/pipeline-sim/deepen.mjs`, and runs the dry-run emit with the rotation on, at a cap of 100 and off (fifteen payloads byte for byte equal to the off run).

### `tests/flows-guard-lexicon.mjs`

The forecast lexicon of the answer guard, with no server and no browser. `FORECAST_ALT` in `shared/flows-ask.js` is the one source of the future-claim words (`will`, `should`, `shall`, `expect*`, `likely` and `unlikely`, `going to`, `gonna`, `forecast*`, `predict*`, `anticipat*`, `forese*`, `poised`, `target*`, `odds` except `at odds`, `imminent`, `inevitabl*`, `ought to`, `due for`, `in store`, `on course` and `on pace to`, `is` or `are about to` and their contractions, and every `'ll`, `won't` and `shan't`), `guardText` normalises the text before any lexicon reads it (curly apostrophes become straight, fullwidth digits ASCII, combining marks and format characters are stripped so a dotted capital I, an accent or a zero-width character inside a word cannot hide it; Cyrillic homoglyphs are a known limit), and the reading vet builds its anchored and any-word forms from the same source. A table of 22 future claims is refused in both apostrophe forms, 11 descriptive sentences (including `at odds with` and `was about to`) pass, and eight planted wrong builds (contractions dropped, no normalisation, a word removed, `at odds` counted) must each fail.
