### `shared/flows-oracle.js`, `shared/flows-oracle-words.js`, `shared/flows-oracle-live.js`, `server/routes/flows-oracle.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/oracle.mjs`, `scripts/flows-legs/oracle-norm.mjs`, `scripts/flows-nightly/sections/oracle.mjs`, `docs/oracle.md`, `tests/flows-oracle.mjs`, `tests/flows-oracle-live.mjs`, `tests/server-oracle.mjs`, `tests/lib/oracle-fixtures.mjs`

The Oracle, the Neuron's always-on view of each name (design in `docs/oracle.md`). `shared/flows-oracle.js` is a pure leaf that turns a card and its engine block into the stored record `oracle:<T>` (state, stance, at most three cheap or rich calls, context, withheld reasons, limits), with every sentence from `shared/flows-oracle-words.js` (no forecast, modal or advice word, and the model-implied footer on every call). A call comes only from a single-leg structure the engine published, and only when its edge clears the pre-registered bars. `COMPUTE_JOBS` now holds `ORACLE_JOB`; the isolated `oracle-publish` stage publishes `oracle:<T>`, the `oracle-top` ranking and the `oracle-norm` ledger (`FLOWS_ORACLE_BUILD=off` skips it). `GET /api/flows/oracle` (`?t=`, `?ts=`, `?top=1`) is a row in the route table behind `FLOWS_ORACLE` (`on`, `owner` or off, fail closed). `shared/flows-oracle-live.js` is the reference leaf of the seven live-flow detectors and the source of the generated bundle `assets/js/flows-oracle-live.bundle.js`; no page, Worker module or script loads either (`tests/flows-oracle-core.mjs` scans for it), and the detectors the pages run are the copy inside `assets/js/flows-oracle-core.js`, which differs in the cluster rule, the lean's stated-sides test and the sweep window, so a threshold is changed in both.

### `scripts/research/flow-registry.json`, `scripts/research/flow-registry.mjs`, `tests/flows-flow-registry.mjs`

The flow signal registry (F-34), committed before any outcome is seen. `scripts/research/flow-registry.json` holds ten signals (Alert activity S1, Screen activity S2, Alert direction with sign S3, burst on vendor alerts S4, burst on large prints S4p, flow turned S5, live opening bound S6, the vendor's sweep-and-volume-over-open-interest rule S7 and unusual preset S8 as benchmarks, contract-level unusual volume S10), each with a live series beside its reconstruction (S1r and so on) that are never pooled, three horizons (the flag's publication to its session's close, the next close, five sessions) and so thirty tests, each pinned to an event target of `shared/flows-targets.js` on the typical-day scale at k = 3 (an up and a down target for a signed signal; the vendor's implied move and any model-owned sigma are refused), the table of targets pinned by version and hash, the five-rule protocol (returns start at the flag's publication, fits see only the past, the universe is the one as of each date, a live series is never pooled with its reconstruction, every name-day of the universe is evaluated), Benjamini-Hochberg at 0.05 across the thirty once per regime and Holm for S1 at the next close across S1's three horizons. `scripts/research/flow-registry.mjs` loads and validates it (a result, a stale targets table, a flipped protocol rule or a target outside F-11 is a problem). `tests/flows-flow-registry.mjs` pins each test's definition hash (a change of definition is a new id) and runs six leaks, each the registered protocol with one rule flipped, on a seeded null history: the registered protocol holds its 5%, and the return measured from the window start, a fit that sees the test day, today's universe applied to old dates, a live series pooled with its reconstruction, evaluation on outcome-selected days and on flagged days only each reject at least 90%.

### `shared/flows-vendor-extras.js`, `scripts/flows-legs/extras.mjs`, `tests/flows-vendor-extras.mjs`, `tests/gen-extras-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-vendor-extras.json`, `docs/vendor-coverage.md`

The vendor extras (Frontier lane 2, design in `docs/vendor-coverage.md`): per-name data the nightly now stores for every name it reads, so the dossier, the reading and Ask hold it without a member's vendor call. `shared/flows-vendor-extras.js` is a leaf over `flows-cross.js`, `flows-freshness.js` and `flows-vendor-core.js`. `PARTS` names eleven parts in two card-x sections: `dx` holds the seven slow dossier sources (`info`, `profile`, `financials`, `breakdown`, `estimates`, `analysts`, `ownership`) reduced by the same reducers the Worker uses, and `vx` holds four operations the spec documents and no code read before (`dividends`, `splits`, `etf` for ETF exposure, `ivp` for interpolated implied volatility). Every part is `{at, rv, x}` (read time, reducer version, extract) or a stub `{at, rv, shed: 1}`; `partState` is fresh inside `refreshS`, stale inside `maxS`, expired beyond it, outdated on a version mismatch, and a part that fails keeps the good one inside its maximum age (`mergeParts`). `planSlow` orders the due parts by how close they are to their maximum age (a missing or outdated one first, ties by a hash of ticker and part) under a per-night cap of 2,000 calls (`SLOW_CAP`, `FLOWS_EXTRAS_CAP`; `FLOWS_EXTRAS=off` turns the leg off); a 40-night simulation on 700 and 856 names holds the cold start to five nights and no part to a read past its maximum age. The reducers name their failures (`unshaped`, `type:<field>`, `empty`), keep dates as dates, and withhold every amount, factor, weight and volatility whose unit the schema does not state (`UNIT_STATUS`). `exercisePressure` is the early-assignment window for a short call: an ex-date after today and on or before the expiry flags it, an ex-date of today does not, one on expiry day does, the last exercise day is the previous trading day from the calendar, and an empty list is never read as no dividend. `fitSection` sheds a section to its budget as stubs, `shedNextPart` does the same one part at a time against the 60 KiB card-x ceiling in `SHED_ORDER`, a fresh stub is not read again before its maximum age, and `shapeHealth` rates a part's failures (above 5 per cent warn, above 20 fail, under 50 attempts report only). `scripts/flows-legs/extras.mjs` is the nightly leg: it reads each owned name's prior card-x, reads the due parts through the pool and the deadline, cleans descriptions with the dossier sanitiser, files failures by status (429 limited, 401, 402, 403, 404 and 422 plan and stored so they are not asked for every night, anything else failed) and hands `runMarketLegs` the merged sections for the card-x store, whose shed order now puts `dx` and `vx` first. In the Worker, `nightlyView` offers a usable `dx` part to `planFetches` as a cache part that is a hit for its own refresh age and never replaces a newer cache row, and the events and peers packets print the dividend, split, ETF and implied-volatility-percentile facts with the amount, factor and weight held back. `tests/gen-extras-fixtures.py` writes `tests/fixtures-vendor-extras.json` from the committed spec (synthetic, invented tickers, marked as not observed on the wire). `tests/flows-vendor-extras.mjs` holds all of it with 16 planted wrong builds of the leaf and 7 of the health gate, the leg, the Worker and the packet. `extrasChecks` in `scripts/flows-legs/health.mjs` carries `shapeHealth` into the nightly gate (a part above the failing line fails the night under the `extras-shape` code only when `extrasStanding` shows seven clean nights behind it and no failure the night before, otherwise it warns; one between the lines warns, one under 50 attempts is a note) and `meta.extras` records the night's calls, due, deferred and unread priors.

### `shared/flows-sides.js`, `shared/flows-round.js`, `tests/flows-sides.mjs`, `tests/flows-round.mjs`

The premium-side and controlled-rounding leaves of the Premium ledger. `shared/flows-sides.js` imports only `flows-tape-stats.js`: `centsOf` reads a vendor decimal exactly into integer cents (a string, or a number by its own digits, half to even, never a float multiply), `windowEntry` books one flagged window as bought (ask side), sold (bid side) and the remainder as reason-coded side-unknown premium (`btw` between the quotes, `cross` a floor trade, `leg` spread legs, `ns` a side the vendor did not report), a window whose stated sides exceed its premium is `invalid`, excluded and counted and never clamped or split pro rata, `leanRead` is the pooled ratio of sums with proven bounds `lo = A/T` and `hi = (A + U)/T`, shown only from 40% stated coverage and $250k stated (`LEAN_WHY` names each gate), `printEntry` does the same for one print, and `askShareCompat` equals `askShareRead` of `shared/flows-tape-stats.js` on the rows both accept. `shared/flows-round.js` imports nothing: `roundTable` rounds a zero-sum table to a display unit so every displayed row and column still foots, by integer minimum-cost flow on the table extended with its margins (no cell moves by a whole unit, which largest remainder cannot promise for two margins at once), and `footsRounded` checks the result. `tests/flows-sides.mjs` holds the ingest strings, the entry table, the lean gates from both sides and the compatibility with the existing ask share; `tests/flows-round.mjs` runs 10,000 random tables against the bound.

### `shared/flows-sfc.js`, `shared/flows-sfc-time.js`, `shared/flows-sfc-tide.js`, `shared/flows-sfc-display.js`, `shared/flows-sfc-stock.js`, `shared/flows-sfc-copy.js`, `shared/flows-sfc-view.js`, `server/routes/flows-ledger.js`, `tests/flows-sfc.mjs`, `tests/flows-sfc-view.mjs`, `tests/flows-sfc-route.mjs`, `tests/lib/sfc-*.mjs`, `tests/lib/copy-lint.mjs`

The Premium ledger (`/flows/ledger/`): a closed accounting ledger of the option premium in the live record's held windows, with liquidity providers as an explicit column so every row and every column sums to zero, and no entry between sectors. `shared/flows-sfc.js` books windows into per-name books in integer cents (`bookWindows`: dedupe by `alertKey` with the later read winning, the New York trade date of `spanStart` as the session, `BigInt` for delta-dollars), files names under the eleven sectors, `Index & ETF` (a fixed ticker table that never feeds a sector's weight) and `No sector` (`readUniverse`), builds the 11 by 6 matrix (`buildMatrix`: four taker rows, four reason-coded side-unknown rows, two outside rows that exist only for sectors that have a tide and are absent, never zero, otherwise, and a balancing entry defined to close each column) and the tilt (`tiltOf`: delta-dollars `X_k` per sector (the taker share of each window's contracts times 100 times a Black-Scholes delta at 4% and window-end volatility times the stock price), allocated against market-cap weights by exact largest remainder with ties to column order and sign symmetry, `R_k = X_k - a_k` summing to zero, a proven determination band `W_k` from the side-unknown premium and the windows without a delta, carrying every other sector's uncertainty through the total (`W_k = (1 - s_k) U_k + s_k (U - U_k)`), verdicts Above share, Below share, Within band and At share, withheld below 80% delta coverage). `shared/flows-sfc-time.js` is the period plan in TRT (Intraday within 30 minutes of a live read, Session, and 5 sessions, which nothing archives and which say so) and the exact five-session sum; `shared/flows-sfc-tide.js` reads the sector tide against the session and the read time; `shared/flows-sfc-display.js` picks one display unit and applies the controlled rounding; `shared/flows-sfc-stock.js` is the stock identity for open interest, tested and not wired because no open interest is stored; `shared/flows-sfc-copy.js` holds every reader-facing sentence; `shared/flows-sfc-view.js` assembles the answer (ledger, sector and name tables, flow arrows, checks, lean, reading) and fails closed: a ledger that does not close is a failed page with no figures and a logged hash, a tilt that does not sum to zero is withheld everywhere it would show. `server/routes/flows-ledger.js` is the `GET /api/flows/ledger` row (scope `all`, `sector:<name>` or `name:<ticker>`, period `intraday`, `session` or `five`) behind the Flows session: one statement for the nightly alerts and the universe, one for the two live rows, at most six rows read, thirty seconds in the Cache API, a failed view never kept, a closure failure logged once. `tests/flows-sfc.mjs` holds production against an independent BigInt oracle (`tests/lib/sfc-oracle.mjs`, written from the decimal strings) over 2,000 seeded worlds with path-count floors, the invariants over 400 worlds, eleven metamorphic relations (the last: an unknown-side window moves no tilt and widens every band by its own share of the new uncertainty), and 40 planted wrong builds (`tests/lib/sfc-plants.mjs`, loaded as relinked copies by `tests/lib/sfc-variant.mjs`), each of which must fail its own named check by an assertion and not a crash; `tests/flows-sfc-view.mjs` holds display, time, stock, scope parsing, the states, absent against zero, the fail-closed paths and the copy lint (`tests/lib/copy-lint.mjs`: no unsided, no tape, no sector-transfer wording, nothing leaving, no forecast word, sentences of at most 14 words) over every string of the view and the copy; `tests/flows-sfc-route.mjs` drives the row.

### `server/desk-sc.js`, `shared/flows-premium-rank.js`, `shared/flows-quant-rank.js`, `shared/flows-quant-forward.js`, `shared/flows-quant-bracket.js`, `tests/flows-premium-rank.mjs`, `tests/server-desk-sc.mjs`, `tests/flows-desk-sc.mjs`, `tests/flows-forward.mjs`, `tests/flows-forward-lab.mjs`, `tests/lib/forward-lab.mjs`, `tests/lib/sc-chain.mjs`, `tests/lib/sc-world.mjs`, `tests/lib/crr.mjs`, `tests/flows-decisions-closed.mjs`

The Premium Desk's server-side ranking and forward (decisions 1 and 2). `strategy=sc` on `GET /api/flows/chain` ranks the whole chain once and sends only the rows that can matter. `server/desk-sc.js` is the I/O half: `gatherPages` reads the first page of 500 contracts and then waves of four, to `SC_PAGES_DEFAULT` (12) pages or the owner switch `FLOWS_DESK_SC_PAGES` (1 to `SC_MAX_PAGES`, 24), keeps the rows already read when the site's vendor budget refuses a later call (and says so) and reports a chain cut by the ceiling as truncated; `chainCacheGet` and `chainCachePut` keep the last two names' reads for 60 s in the isolate so another set of terms is a re-rank and not a vendor call, a refresh drops them, a partial read is never kept; `singleFlight` shares one read between simultaneous callers. `shared/flows-premium-rank.js` is the pure half: `leanRows` removes other roots, junk symbols and duplicates (the better quote wins, deterministically), `eligibleCall` is `gateOf`, the gate `rankChain` uses too, so the two callers cannot disagree, `preRank` keys every call under shares held, delta-adjusted and Reg-T style collateral with `shortCallLine` (the delta is the one the page's engine computes: `leanDelta` equals it to the last digit on every sampled row; a call whose forward is too uncertain has no delta and no delta key) and keeps the top `RANK_K` (25) of each convention in each of five expiry buckets (`shared/flows-quant-rank.js`: 1 to 7, 8 to 14, 15 to 42, 43 to 90 and 91 to 400 days, every one wholly inside one of the page's tenor windows, so the union of the bucket lists holds each tenor's top 25 exactly), shrinking k to fit `RANK_CAP` (480) rows; `lineCompare` is the one tie order (key, open interest, strike, expiry, symbol) the server and the page share through the bundle; `rankShortCalls` prices the kept rows, marks each with the bitmask `pk` of the conventions that picked it (1 shares, 2 delta, 4 Reg-T), attaches `br`, the model-free bracket of its win chance from the chain's own call spreads, and returns `cut` (contracts read, calls, eligible, sent, keyed by convention, k, buckets, terms, spot, as-of, duplicates, calls without a delta, calls whose forward is too uncertain, pages, truncated, and what was shed and why). Under 150 ms of CPU for 12,000 rows. Decision 2: `shared/flows-quant-forward.js` chooses each expiry's forward. `carryOf` is the card's carry with its error (rate 2% and borrow 3% a year, a tenth of the dividend drift, or 1.5% a year when the yield is unknown); `selectPairs` takes up to 12 clean call-put pairs inside 5% of the carry forward (widening to 15% and 8 pairs when fewer than three), with a relative spread of at most 25% and a call delta from 0.30 to 0.70 (the upper cut only where dividends may be paid); `parityOf` weighs the pairs' forwards by the inverse square of the quote error (half-spread over the root of three, half a cent at least), inflates by the Birge ratio and adds 15% of the strike times one minus the discount for American exercise and reports the slope of the pairs' forwards against the strike as a t-statistic (`slopeZ`, four pairs or more); `chooseForward` blends parity with the carry, widening the carry's error by what the disagreement cannot be explained by (an empirical-Bayes variance) and adding the card's spot error (1.5 bp). Parity below the carry by two to four errors, with the pairs' forwards falling with the strike by two to four errors (both tests ramp from 0 to 1), is the signature of early exercise rather than of a borrow fee or a special dividend, whose pairs are flat in the strike: the parity forward is then moved away from the carry by a quarter of the gap and its error widened by 15% of the gap, scaled by the ramp, and with both tests past three errors the record is flagged `ee`, on which the card reads a call's volatility from the matching put quote and the page withholds the call-side bracket. The label is `parity` when the carry keeps under 5% of the weight and `blend` otherwise, and a forward that fewer than three pairs contradict by four errors is a veto. `deltaSensitivity` and `deltaBand` turn the forward's error into the delta's: plain up to 0.02, plus-minus up to 0.10 rounded up, an em dash beyond; `dDeltaMax` bounds the sensitivity over every strike. The page and the Worker price a line the same way: the volatility is re-solved from the out-of-the-money mid under the chosen forward (`contractFit`) and the time to expiry is the elapsed minutes over a 365-day year. `shared/flows-quant-bracket.js` bounds the digital price at a barrier from call spreads at or above the forward (puts below it) over all pairs of strikes, and `winRange` and `outsideRange` turn it into the range the page compares the model's win chance with (a point of tolerance, plus the discount for the put side). The suites: `flows-premium-rank` (500 seeds: caps, gates, determinism under reordering, the pre-rank delta equal to the page engine, the per-tenor and per-bucket top 25 equal to a full-chain truth, the regression chain whose number one sat on page three, CPU, and 13 planted wrong builds), `server-desk-sc` (paging, caches, single flight, 10 planted builds), `flows-desk-sc` (the route in the Worker against a stub vendor and eight planted builds), `flows-forward` (worked examples against an independent implementation, finite differences, boundaries, brackets against Black-76, American puts by binomial tree, the early-exercise ramp, shift, flag and put-side read against an independent reference over a grid of levels and slopes, 44 planted builds of the module and others of the card and the premium module) and `flows-forward-lab` (the calibration lab: liquid and illiquid chains at three hours and one, two, seven and thirty days under a correct card, a hard-to-borrow name, a special dividend and an American ex-dividend window; coverage of the forward by how far the chain sits from the carry, the accuracy and the sharpness of printed delta bands per chain, the bracket against the true digital, the flag firing on American chains and never on European ones, with 13 planted builds, each criterion holding on seven seeds (`FORWARD_LAB_SEED` reruns another) and each build failing on every one; the three-hour American chain and the illiquid American chains are printed and not asserted, because a dividend the card does not list cannot be seen from them). Decisions closed: `FLOWS_DESK_SC_PAGES=0` turns the Short calls server ranking off (the route answers the plain covered-call chain stamped `scOff`); a member may force three `refresh=1` re-reads of the Short calls snapshot a minute, the rest are served from it; `carryOf` takes `nEx`, the count of dated ex-dividends from card-x `vx.dividends` inside the horizon, and with a known yield scales the drift by it (`datedYield`), leaving an unknown yield unknown and an empty window unchanged, never zero. `tests/flows-decisions-closed.mjs` holds these with planted wrong builds and holds `docs/math-audit.md` to six closed decisions.

### `shared/flows-calendar.js`, `shared/flows-horizon.js`, `tests/flows-calendar.mjs`, `tests/gen-calendar-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-calendar.json`, `tests/flows-horizon.mjs`, `tests/flows-horizon-lint.mjs`

The one horizon in trading sessions, and the calendar it is counted on. `shared/flows-horizon.js` is an import-free leaf: `HORIZON_SESSIONS` (21, the only place the number is written), `RD_MAX` (99), `AHEAD_SESSIONS` (5), `REACTION_BACK_SESSIONS` (1), `LIVE_ELAPSED_MAX` (2, the most sessions a page places a live session on a nightly), `earningsState(row, hz, j, back)` and `earningsInside` (the one predicate that says whether a name's earnings are `reaction` (its move is the event's), `after` (the day after), `unstated` (a report time not stated, so the page cannot say whether the move is the event's), `ahead` (a report inside the next `hz` sessions), `clear` or `undated`, from `ed`, `rd`, `rdMax` and `edMax`, with `j` the sessions elapsed since the universe's session, which has no default, so a page can age a nightly row; a value that is not a whole number is no age, never zero. Every report is two candidate reaction sessions, one when its time is stated: a report with a stated time is `rd` or `ed` alone, an unstated one behind the session is `rd` of the later candidate beside `rdMax` of the earlier, and an unstated one ahead is `ed` of the earlier candidate beside `edMax`, which the universe carries in `edUnstated` beside `edUnstatedThrough` (`ed` 0 is an unstated-time report dated on the universe's session and has the candidates 0 and 1, and an `edMax` equal to `ed` says the time is stated); `laterCandidate(map, through, ticker, ed)` and the kernel's `F.later` give a name's `edMax`: the map's value, none for a name inside the horizon the map is complete through, and `ed + 1` for one beyond it or from a payload that states no horizon, so an unlisted report is never taken as stated by default; the later candidate of an unstated report behind the session counts as ahead while it has not yet happened. Each candidate is aged by `j` and read as reaction, after (within `back`, default `REACTION_BACK_SESSIONS`) or clear, and a report whose candidates disagree is `unstated`; the precedence is reaction, then unstated, then after, then the tail (`ahead` when the next report is 1 to `hz` sessions from the aged session, else `clear`, `undated` for a name with no date). `EVENT_STATES` says which states set a name apart: all but `clear` and `undated`. A payload with no window or a nightly too old to place gives no state at all, which a reader must treat as unplaced and never as clear: the Moves page does not test such a name) and the copy words built from a horizon the payload names. `shared/flows-calendar.js` wraps the rule-derived NYSE holiday and early-close sets of `flows-quant-time.js` (one source of truth) over `CALENDAR_FIRST` to `CALENDAR_LAST` (2020-01-01 to 2027-12-31; any day outside is null, never a guess): `isSession`, `sessionIndex`, `sessionsBetween(from, to)` (signed, any days), `addSessions(day, n)` (n may be negative), `nextSession`, `previousSession`, `horizonEnd(asOf)`, `ahead(asOf)` (the five session days after it, which a page counts a live session against because pages carry no holiday table), `impactSession(reportDate, reportTime)` (the one definition in `flows-targets.js`, handed the session list, wrapped as `{early, late, day, certain}`: before the open the first session on or after the date, after the close the first session after it, a date that is not a session the next session whatever its time, an unstated time the interval between the two), `forward` (ed: sessions from the universe's session to the nearer impact, 0 only for a report of unstated time dated that session, null for a report already behind it; `certain` false when the time is not stated, and its later candidate is `ed + 1`), `backward` (rd of the later candidate with `rdMax` of the earlier) and `withinHorizon`. `coverage(now)` says whether the table reaches now plus `CALENDAR_LEAD_DAYS` (120, the horizon in calendar days rounded up and 90 more, so it follows `HORIZON_SESSIONS`) and warns from `CALENDAR_WARN_DAYS` (365) before it, and the suite fails on the day the lead is not there. `tests/gen-calendar-fixtures.py` writes `tests/fixtures-calendar.json` from `exchange_calendars` (XNYS, with both bounds passed, because its default end is a year ahead): the holidays and early closes of 2020 to 2027, the impact session of 40 dates under each time, 1,275 session counts, offsets and indexes, with the package version, the generator's sha256 and the session list's hash. `tests/flows-calendar.mjs` holds every day of the table to that oracle, the three session counters in the tree to one answer, the boundary tables of the horizon (a Monday, the Thursday before Good Friday, the Wednesday before Thanksgiving, with the report before the open, after the close and unstated), the live adjustment, the candidate model against a truth read off `impactSession` itself (12 sessions, report dates from 30 days back to 45 ahead, four times, six elapsed counts, two windows and three reaction windows) and the lead and warning days, and the invariants the readers rely on (ed 0 only for an unstated report dated on the session, a report ahead or behind and never both, rdMax equal to rd for a certain report and one more for an uncertain one) over 300 sessions, and fails 16 planted wrong builds (five of them break an invariant; weekends only, a closed Friday removed, an off-by-one floor, the horizon one session short or long, the earlier or later candidate taken for the forward count, a report behind the session counted as ahead, the weekday-only impact mode back, a table ending early). `tests/flows-horizon.mjs` holds the page kernel's copy of the earnings state, inside test, elapsed-session count and window sentence (`FlowsUI.F.earn`, `earnIn`, `elapsed` and `horizon` in `assets/js/flows-ui.js`, the one browser copy, which every page reads its event flag from; `elapsed` finds the session of the data shown by its place in the universe's `ahead` list, and is null for a day older than the universe, not listed or more than two sessions on) equal to the leaf over 218,790 cells and to the calendar's own reaction sessions, with 32 planted wrong builds, a table of 13 later-candidate cases with 9 more planted builds, and a scan that every page reading the map reads its horizon and goes through `F.later`. `tests/flows-horizon-lint.mjs` is the grep test: no source file outside `shared/flows-horizon.js` holds the earnings horizon as a number (every other 21 is a named, unrelated window with a count per file, so a new one fails), no distance to a report is compared with a number, and every constant for an earnings window or gate is on a reviewed list.

### `shared/flows-reports.js`, `tests/flows-reports.mjs`

The rd column's pure half: sessions since a name's last earnings report, with the impact session of each report (`flows-calendar.js`) as its day. `observeHistory` takes the latest report in a name's `/api/earnings/{ticker}` rows (a row is a report when its source is `company` or it carries an actual figure or a measured move; an estimation row with neither is not), `observeCalendar` takes a date-scoped premarket or after-hours calendar read (the symbol field, with the route giving the time), `timingOf` reads premarket, bmo, postmarket and amc case-insensitively and everything else as not stated, `span` gives the two sessions an unstated time allows, `rdOf` answers `{rd, why}` with rd the whole number of sessions from the impact session to the universe's session (0 on the reaction session itself) or null with the reason `none`, `timing` (a report time not stated: no rd, only `rdMax`, how old it can be at most), `over` (more than 99) or `unconfirmed`, never zero and never a default. `pick` and `mergeObservations` decide between two sightings of one report (two within 10 sessions of each other are one report; a stated time beats an unstated one, tonight's calendar beats a ledger entry written from a calendar beats tonight's history beats a ledger entry written from history; between two reports the later wins) and log each disagreement. `mergeLedger` folds tonight's sightings into the prior ledger for every name in the universe, drops names that left and entries over 99 sessions old, and marks a name `unconfirmed` when the next report date the previous night saw fell inside a stretch no reaction read covered. `reactionPlan(sessionDate, ledgerAsOf)` is the date-scoped reads: the session's premarket and the previous SESSION's after-hours (not the previous weekday, which after a holiday asks for a closed day), and the same pair for each session of a skipped night, up to 5; `coveredDays` names a day covered only when both of its reads answered below the 100 row limit. `encodeLedger` and `decodeLedger` are the wire form kept under the `reports` key (sorted tickers, calendar-day offsets back from the ledger's day, one code for time and origin, the next date as an offset forward; a wrong version reads as empty and never throws) and `basisOf` is the small basis the universe carries beside its rd column. `recentReporters(universe)` is the Events page's reacted-lately block: it runs the same `earningsState` the Moves page runs over the universe's `ed`, `rd` and the sparse `rdBasis.unstated` ages, so its two counts (the reaction session and the one before) are the Moves page's two sets, names whose report time is not stated are counted apart (`unstated`, null when a cut report date touched the two ages), a count whose reaction session a cut report date could touch is null with the ages listed in `cut`, the names in their reaction session are ranked by the move in the name's own typical days (`ln(1 + chg) / sig`, a missing typical day last, never a zero), and the names that reacted two to five sessions ago (`laterRows`, entries `[ticker, age, flag]`: the age is `rd`, or for a report of unstated time its later candidate `rdMax - 1`, and the flag is 1 for that case, so the page says the time is not stated and the name may be a session older) are a separate list with no move, since the universe holds only the last session's change. The status is `ok` or `quiet`, or `unavailable` with `universe_unavailable`, `withheld_budget`, `not_published` or `no_window`. `tests/flows-reports.mjs` holds the rules to an independent count of sessions from the exchange's holiday list (a premarket report on a Monday, after the close on a Friday, before Thanksgiving and Good Friday, on a Saturday and on Presidents Day, an unstated time, rd 99 and 100), the precedence cases, the skipped night, a cut calendar day, universe churn and the wire round trip, and fails 44 planted wrong builds (the ledger and `recentReporters`, the forward map with its cap and horizon among them, and three of the horizon's).

### `shared/flows-card-pack.js`, `tests/flows-card-pack.mjs`

How a card-x body travels: the headroom the next field needs, behind a pipeline switch that ships off. `shared/flows-card-pack.js` is an import-free leaf. A card-x holds about two hundred day lists under the key `d` (the vol risk premium, skew, anomaly, sentiment and character series, the short and insider rows and each contract row), 13 bytes a day as `"2026-08-24"`; `packCardX` writes each ascending list of three or more real calendar days as `d0` (the first day) beside `dd` (the calendar-day gaps), puts the marker `pk` first, and returns the same object when there is nothing to pack, when the document already carries the marker, or when it holds a key the format reserves (`pk`, `d0`, `dd`, `__proto__`). `unpackCardX` is its exact inverse, key order included. `packText` and `unpackText` do the same on text and fail safe: `packText` unpacks what it just packed and posts the document whole if the round trip is not the posted text, and `unpackText` answers null for a `{"pk":` text it cannot decode or whose version it does not know. `wireText(key, text, on)` is the pipeline's side: the text packed for a `card-x:` key only when `on` is exactly true, else the text. `decodeWire(key, text)` is the Worker's: for a `card-x:` key whose text starts with `{"pk":` it answers the document text, or an error when it does not decode or the version is unknown, and for every other key and every other body the text unchanged. `wireBytes` is the UTF-8 size of the packed document with the five-digit `bytes` field the Worker adds, `servedBytes` the size unpacked. The packing is a wire format and not a storage format: `server/routes/flows-ingest.js` and the door's `?op=batch` decode a packed card-x body before anything else looks at it (before the JSON check, the content refusal, the door's inspection and the content hash), refuse one that does not decode as `400 invalid_payload`, and apply the 128 KiB cap to the decoded text too (`413 payload_too_large`), so the D1 row, every reader, the dossier's `json_extract` and the door's canonical hash are exactly what an unpacked post gives, and no reader, store function or `wrangler.toml` variable knows about it. `scripts/flows-nightly/store.mjs` sends a card-x packed only while `FLOWS_CARDX_WIRE` is exactly `pack` (unset or anything else: posted whole) and only after the fixture check; the dry run's emit stays whole. `scripts/flows-legs/card-x.mjs` counts the soft limit (`CARD_X_SOFT_BYTES`, 60 KiB) with `cardXBytes`, the wire size while the switch is on and the served size when it is off, and in both cases keeps the served size under `CARD_X_SERVED_BYTES` (90% of the 100 KiB budget), so a document the packing makes small on the wire cannot grow past the room the card beside it needs in the Worker. On the dry-run emit's 175 cards the largest is 61,408 bytes as posted today (21 shed their extras to stay under the limit) and 56,307 bytes on the wire with the switch on (91.6% of the soft limit, under the 95% line of 58,368; none sheds; 62,423 bytes served). `tests/flows-card-pack.mjs` holds a known answer, the refusals (two days, unsorted, an impossible day, a timestamp, a null, reserved keys, a nested `__proto__`), 600 seeded random documents, the 175 emitted cards, the digest of the packed bytes identical in six time zones from UTC-11 to UTC+14, a second emit with the switch on (the largest on the wire, no card shed, the served ceiling), the fit under both settings (including a 5,000-day card whose wire size fits and whose served size would not), the pipeline's send (packed after the fixture check, never in the dry run), the Worker end to end (a packed post stored as the document and byte for byte the row of a whole post, the same bytes and content hash from every reader, the door off, warn and unset, an undecodable body and an unknown version refused, a body of another key that starts with the marker stored as it is, a decoded body past the cap refused, the batch item by item) and 36 planted wrong builds.

### `shared/flows-display-round.js`, `shared/flows-event-variance.js`, `tests/math-format.mjs`, `tests/math-units.mjs`, `tests/math-dealer.mjs`, `tests/math-time.mjs`, `tests/math-print.mjs`, `tests/math-darkpool.mjs`, `tests/math-short.mjs`, `tests/math-sigma.mjs`, `tests/math-vol.mjs`, `tests/math-duplicates.mjs`, `tests/gen-math-*.py`, `tests/lib/ui-boot.mjs`, `tests/fixtures-math-*.json`, `docs/math-audit.md`

The mathematical-correctness audit (lane 2 of the 2026-10-26 programme) and its oracles; `docs/math-audit.md` is the table of formula, production file, oracle, result and fix. `shared/flows-display-round.js` is the one rounding rule for every figure: round half away from zero on the shortest round-trip decimal of the double (`fixedHalfUp`, which moves the decimal point in the number's text instead of multiplying, so `pct(1.005, 0)` is 101%), no minus on a zero, a nonzero value that rounds to zero printed as its floor (`<0.1%`, `−<$1`) and never as zero, float noise under a nano taken as an exact zero, and the unit of a compact figure chosen after rounding (`formatScaled`: 999,950 is `1.00M`, never `1000K`; K, M, B and T). `assets/js/flows-ui.js` carries the same nine functions by copy, because the browser imports nothing, and `tests/math-format.mjs` holds the two copies equal in source and in output and holds both to `tests/fixtures-math-format.json`, 17,005 cases written by `tests/gen-math-format-fixtures.py` from Python `decimal` (ROUND_HALF_UP) and an independent mirror of the unit policy; it also plants fifteen wrong builds and ratchets the count of local rounding sites per file against `tests/fixtures-math-format-sites.json` (a count that rises fails, and one that falls fails until the baseline is lowered with `--write-sites`). `tests/math-units.mjs` holds the five server-side compact-money formatters (the card's `saidMagnitude`, the Neuron's `magnitude`, the dossier's `compact`, the Oracle's `usdShort` and the variation sayer's money) to the same rollover rule, with the number pinned for the guard equal to the number printed. `tests/math-dealer.mjs` holds the closed-form delta, gamma, vanna and charm to 50-digit mpmath at spots from 8.25 to 1450 (never 100, where per-1% and per-dollar coincide), the sticky-strike gamma profile to 1e-9 of its peak, every zero crossing to 1e-9 of spot (the flip is polished by the Illinois method on the closed-form profile instead of interpolated inside its 0.25% cell, which was out by up to 0.016 at a spot of 1450), and the put-sign vote of `bookLevels` by dollars (a flipped row under 1% of the put dollars is tolerated, above it the book is mixed, whatever the row count); `tests/gen-math-dealer-fixtures.py` writes the fixture. `tests/math-time.mjs` holds the hand-written NYSE calendar, early closes, the US daylight rule, the Eastern day and the TRT open and close of every session since 2017 to `exchange_calendars` and the IANA zones (`tests/gen-math-time-fixtures.py`), and the closing print to its New York session whatever its TRT date. `tests/lib/ui-boot.mjs` boots `flows-ui.js` in a `vm` with optional exact-once source patches for planted builds. `tests/math-print.mjs` holds the option-print classifier to exact fractions on integer ticks (6,845 triples from `tests/gen-math-print-fixtures.py`; a fill at a locked quote has no side and says why, a zero bid stays a quote) and plants eleven wrong builds. `tests/math-darkpool.mjs` holds the off-exchange panel, the Market list and the cross-feed position to Decimal and Fraction over 60 sets of invented prints (`tests/gen-math-darkpool-fixtures.py`): a canceled print is left out of every sum, share and rank, counted, and listed last and tagged. `tests/math-short.mjs` holds the five-session short-volume ratio (pooled sums, with its basis named) and the z against the prior sixty sessions to Fraction and 50-digit Decimal (`tests/gen-math-short-fixtures.py`). `tests/math-sigma.mjs` holds the typical day (1.4826 times the median absolute deviation of the last 252 log returns) and the counts beyond two and three typical days to scipy (`tests/gen-math-sigma-fixtures.py`, written with numpy and scipy from the oracle environment). `tests/math-vol.mjs` holds the volatility and cross-section statistics to numpy, scipy and exact rational algebra (`tests/gen-math-vol-fixtures.py`): realized volatility at 252 sessions and ddof 1, Yang-Zhang, Parkinson, the overnight share, IV rank against percentile (the share at or below, today included), the event move and forward volatility of a term structure, the z-score, Pearson, the average-rank percentile, the quantile, winsorising and the robust z; a constant series has a variance of exactly zero and no correlation, a correlation never leaves minus one to one, and the IV-rank twin of the quant world is held equal to `flows-vol.js`. `shared/flows-event-variance.js` is the one leaf for the event move (`eventTerms`: the front expiry's variance in excess of the diffusive forward variance, T1 T2 (s1 - s2)(s1 + s2) / (T2 - T1), so a flat term structure is exactly no premium and never a zero-sized event move), called by `flows-vol.js` and `flows-quant-smile.js` instead of two copies of the formula. `tests/math-duplicates.mjs` scans the tree for a second normal CDF, erf or inverse normal (the Abramowitz and Stegun constants may appear in no file, the Acklam and Cody tables in `shared/flows-quant-bs.js` alone) and plants a copy of each to prove the scan fails.

### `shared/ff-contract.js`, `shared/ff-schema.js`, `migrations/0026_ff.sql`, `docs/ff/contract.md`, `docs/ff/inventory.md`, `tests/ff-contract.mjs`, `tests/fixtures-ff-contract.json`, `tests/lib/d1-budget-ff.mjs`

The Fire & Forget Phase 0 contract, merged before any lane branches. `shared/ff-contract.js` is a leaf that imports nothing and carries the frozen vocabulary and enums, the health states and rules, the TRT and session-label rules, the closed units and their precision, the metric registry (id, unit, subject, kind, edge, cadence, per-cadence source, range, staleness, baseline, null law, status and reason), the ten templates T01 to T10 with their default wire bodies and house ids, the wire AST v1 grammar and limits, the ids and dedupe rules with the canonical JSON and base32 helpers, the three payload products (`ff-metrics`, `ff-carry`, `ff-calibration`), the routes and pages, the error codes, the browser hooks, slots, events and tokens, the switches, the lanes and the exact file names each owns, the files only lane G may edit, and the imports and calls that evaluation may never make. `shared/ff-schema.js` is the DDL of the sixteen `ff_` tables (wires, immutable versions, hits in a house stream and a stream per member with a gapless sequence, receipts, records, confirmations, runs, switches, the intraday archive) that `migrations/0026_ff.sql`, the FF block of `schema.sql` and the Worker's first-use DDL repeat. `docs/ff/contract.md` is the human contract with the acceptance tests H1 to H15; `docs/ff/inventory.md` is the data inventory that decides which templates ship (five available, two limited, one warming, two hidden). `tests/lib/d1-budget-ff.mjs` declares the four row-budget lines (nightly, live, api, housekeeping) that `tests/lib/d1-budget.mjs` sums. `tests/ff-contract.mjs` holds the contract, the DDL and the docs equal, runs the tables' behaviour (gapless sequence, frozen hits and versions, closed enums) and the wire validator against independent oracles in `tests/fixtures-ff-contract.json`, scans every Fire & Forget file for ownership, comments, colour literals, fixture tickers, vendor imports and calls, and fails 134 planted wrong builds.

## flows-rail

The real-time rail.

### `shared/flows-rt.js`

The real-time rail's pure half: the frozen envelope, topic table, row columns and close codes, the owner switches (`rtSwitches`), `streamEntry` (the `entryOf` shape plus the lag guard), the parse of the 256-byte client messages, per-topic sequence counter and `createSeq` gap detector, the 240-call budget, lag statistics, the latest-wins and append-dedupe merges, the REST shapers `shapePx`, `shapeFl`, `shapeGx`, `shapeMk`, `shapeNw` (vendor body to neutral frame, calling the stored live keys' own shapers), `mergeTopic`, and `RT_UPSTREAM`, the one table from topic to REST request and to vendor socket channels.

### `shared/flows-rt-socket.js`

The vendor socket upstream's pure half (P2-30): the socket URL (the only place the token is put in a URL), the channels a demand wants (per-ticker only, at most eight price and three gex), the ack and data parser, the backoff table, and the shapers that turn a socket payload into the same neutral frame and row the REST shapers give (`patchPxRow` and `mergePxRow` keep a focus name's option-flow columns current under a socket price).

### `shared/flows-rt-hub.js`

`RtHub` (demand, roster, scheduler tick, merge, frames, degrade and resync, hello, status; no REST in it), `createRestUpstream` (the REST adapter: every path, cursor, rotation, deadline and 429 pause lives here), `createSocketUpstream` and `createCompositeUpstream` (the vendor socket upstream behind `FLOWS_RT_UPSTREAM`, shared by every member), `createHub`, `loadRosterFromD1` (one read-only batch) and the `Pulse` Durable Object class wiring hibernatable sockets and alarms to the hub. Imports no `cloudflare:workers`, so Node loads it.

`serveRt`: `/api/rt/ws`, `/api/rt/snap`, `/api/rt/status` inside `route()`; the kill switches, the audience gate, the origin check, and the forward to the one named object.

### `assets/js/flows-rt.js`

The rail's browser half, attached as `FlowsUI.rt`: one WebSocket per tab with refcounted topics, the ladder socket, then `GET /api/rt/snap` every 5 s, then the pages' own heartbeats (and `off` when the rail answers 401, 403 or 404), per-topic sequence and epoch handling (a gap sends `{t:"rs"}` and holds the topic until a snapshot; a new epoch discards its state), `rt:<topic>` freshness entries that are dropped when the transport falls, rAF-conflated listeners (`FlowsUI.rt.on`), and the adapters the pages read: `strips()` (px rows mapped back to the `live:strips` body, `fields` from `meta.cols`), `quote(t)`, `market(base)`, `alerts()`, `news()`, `gex(t)`, `feeds(k)`. It never touches the DOM.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|

### `scripts/flows-legs/stamp.mjs`, `tests/flows-pipeline-golden.mjs`, `tests/fixtures-pipeline-golden.json`, `tests/lib/nightly-emit.mjs`

The nightly's pinned dry-run clock and the golden emit. `FLOWS_DRY_NOW` (an ISO instant ending in `Z`, honoured only with `--dry-run`, anything else throws) pins `stampNow()`/`stampDate()`, the one clock every payload stamp (`generatedAt`, `readAt`, `screenerReadAt`, `refreshedAt`) is read from; elapsed-time and deadline measures keep the wall clock, and the pipeline's own source scan fails on a bare `new Date().toISOString()`. `nightlyEmit()` builds the dry-run emit once (CI default clock `2026-08-24T21:45:00Z`) into `FLOWS_EMIT_DIR`, which `tests/run.mjs` sets to one directory per runner invocation and removes at the end, or into a temporary directory of its own; a mark holding a fingerprint of `scripts/` and `shared/` makes a stale directory rebuild. The eight emit consumers (`flows-card-render`, `flows-events-contract`, `flows-overlay-contract`, `flows-payload-shape`, `flows-positioning-contract`, `flows-strip`, `flows-ticker-contract`, `flows-unusual-contract`) read it instead of running their own. The golden suite (the first half of `test:pipeline`) hashes every emitted payload against the manifest, builds a second emit to prove two runs at one clock are byte-identical, and names the payload and top-level keys that moved; `node flows-pipeline-golden.mjs --write` regenerates the manifest after an intended change and `--against <emit dir of the old tree>` prints the leaf paths.

### `scripts/flows-pipeline.mjs`, `scripts/flows-nightly/*.mjs`, `scripts/flows-nightly/sections/*.mjs`

The nightly. The entry is the command line: the session prelude and the unranked-refresh early return, `runLiveMode`, `main` (one `ctx`, eleven awaited sections), and a re-export of every name the suites import, so no suite imports a module path. The modules sit in layers and an import may only point down: `flags` (the process flags and the `FLOWS_DRY_NOW` pin), `vendor-params` (what is asked of the vendor and the call budget), `vendor` (`uw`, the permit queue, the read pace and the pool), `store` (ingest URL, the live credential, read, publish, retire, prune and the dated-key grammar; `hold` keeps a payload in the run's store without writing it, and `flushHeld` writes each held key that no later write landed, the latest payload first and the held one if that is refused: the vol leg's card-x for every name the flow leg rewrites is held so that one write carries both), `clock`, `rank` (scoring and shaping, `enrich`), `fixtures` (the dry run's synthetic vendor), `archive` (board memory, the dated walk, the record's inputs, the roster ledger), then the sections, `runUniverse` to `runClose` (`runCompute` between `runFocus` and `runClose`), none importing another; `stages` is a leaf. A section destructures what it reads from `ctx` and returns what a later one needs with `Object.assign(ctx, ...)`; `tests/flows-legs-contract.mjs` holds each declaration to its module, the layer rule and the no-comment rule for the whole tree. A test reads the nightly through `nightlySource()` (entry, then every module, each marked), `nightlySlice()` (throws when a slice crosses a module) or `nightlyExecution()` (the entry with each section spliced in where it runs) from `tests/lib/source-scan.mjs`, never by reading one file.

### `scripts/flows-nightly/stages.mjs`

The nightly's stage table (`STAGES`: id, fatal or isolated, `needs`, declared `publishes`) and `createStageRunner`: `step(id)` for a fatal stage, `run(id, work, onError)` for an isolated one (the failure is recorded and handed to `onError`, a fatal one rethrows), `skip`, `note(key)` from `publish`, `detail(fields)` (at most six extra numbers or short strings on the open stage's record, never a record field; `vol-flow` adds `mlNames`, `mlFull`, `mlFirstBad` and `mlLaterBad` only when a multi-leg page failed, and `volFlushed` and `volFailed` only when a held vol payload had to be written on its own), `finish`, `records()`; given a `cpu` clock the runner adds `cpu` (whole milliseconds of the process's CPU time) to every record, and the dry run pins that clock to 0 with the stamp. `meta` is the last write of the nightly, after the health gate, and carries `stages` and `health` (`healthRecord`); `publishedStore.meta` is set earlier so the brief still reads it. The pipeline's pipeline-contract block holds the table to the source (every id once, in order, fatal stages stepped and isolated ones run) and to the dry run (every key declared, `meta` last).

### `scripts/flows-nightly/sections/compute.mjs`

The compute stage, an isolated stage after `card-x` and before `roster`, once the vendor reads are done: it imports no vendor client, store or archive, so it cannot call the vendor or publish. `computeNames` runs the registered `COMPUTE_JOBS` (frozen, holding the Oracle job: `{ id, run(ticker, ctx) }`) over the carded names one at a time with a turn of the event loop between names; a name that spends more than `COMPUTE_NAME_CPU_MS` (1,000) of CPU is named in `over`, the stage stops before the next name once its share (names times that budget) is spent or the run's deadline has passed, and a job that throws costs that name that job only. Values are kept by job and ticker on `ctx.computed` for a later stage to publish. The stage record carries `cpu`, `names`, `jobs`, `over`, `failed` and `stopped`.

### `scripts/flows-ws-probe.mjs`, `.github/workflows/flows-ws-probe.yml`, `tests/flows-ws-probe-contract.mjs`

The vendor socket probe, dispatch only: handshake three ways, one connection per channel from one list led by a negative control (`w03_nonexistent_channel_zz`), five firehoses at 5 s each, concurrent connections, joins per connection, lag against each frame's own stamp; per channel messages and bytes a second, inter-arrival p50/p95 and `created_at - executed_at` for prints. It prints key names and statistics, never payload values or the token, and its start record carries the computed duration the workflow's 30-minute timeout must cover. The contract runs it against a fake vendor that speaks the WebSocket protocol by hand.

### `scripts/uw-spec-diff.mjs`

Reads two copies of the vendor's OpenAPI document in the layout `docs/uw-openapi.yaml` has and names what moved between them: operations added and removed, parameters added, removed or flipped between required and optional, new deprecations (a `deprecated: true` line or a description that says so), changed plan sentences and new changelog entries. `--check` exits 1 on any difference, 2 on a document it cannot read. It prints no response data; `docs/uw-openapi.md` says when to run it.

### `scripts/flows-legs/calibration.mjs`, `tests/flows-calibration.mjs`

The track record of the engine's ideas. The pipeline archives each night's lead idea as a trial (`ideaRecord`: legs, fill, spot, both chances of profit) inside `ideas:<date>`; `runCalibration` reads the earlier dates, settles each identity (ticker, family, legs, expiry) once from the daily close on its expiry with the engine's own payoff and profit intervals, folds the outcome into cluster (ISO expiry week) and reliability accumulators, writes `ideas-out:<date>` then `calib`, and fetches at most 15 closes a night for names that left the deep set. No look-ahead: only dates before the run, bars cut at the run, an expiry on a holiday settles on the last close before it. Calendars and ideas across a split are excluded by name; ideas with no close are counted, never dropped. `calib` shows reliability only from effective n 100. `flows-stats.js` is the P1-51a leaf, byte-identical to the one on its own branch.

## flows-client

Flows pages, scripts and styles.

### `assets/js/flows-fresh.js`

The client freshness helper (`FlowsUI.freshFrom`, `heartbeat`); every key a heartbeat reads registers its server verdict with the pill, which is the worst case over its sources, so a page needs no line per region. The pill says Live only when the market is open, the page has at least one entry registered `role: "primary"` (the rail registers the topics a page connects for: `px` where it asks for it) and every primary entry is inside its own live window with none stale (`FlowsUI.freshAll`, in `flows-ui.js`); the pill repaints itself when that window closes, a page with no primary entry never says Live and reads `Fresh · <age of the newest price read>`, and a `secondary` entry that is not live is listed in the details and demotes the pill to Fresh only when it is stale.

### `assets/js/flows-link.js`, `tests/flows-link.mjs`

`FlowsUI.link`, the one selection and time-cursor mechanism, added to the existing global. `flows-ui.js` carries a queue stub; the first `join` (or any `emit` or `cursor`) loads this body from beside `flows-ui.js` with the same `?v=` and `attach` drains the queue. `join(owner, {channel A, B, C or null, kinds, clocks, on})` returns `{leave}`; `emit(owner, {kind ticker, sector, strike, expiry, side or time, value, source})` is refused unless the value passes the kind's validator; the last emission per channel wins and each member is called at most once per animation frame, never for its own emission; a member that joins late is handed the current selection; focus never moves; announcements go through one polite live region at most every five seconds; `cursor({tau, clock known or event})` carries its clock and a member that lists only the other clock is not handed it and the page is told; `describe()` is `{scene, mode, tau, clock, selection}` in at most 256 bytes. The state is the URL hash `#l=A:t=NVDA;B:s=XLK;tc=known@2026-10-05T14:41:00Z` and nothing else: no browser storage. A second window shares it only when asked: `popout(path)` (a path on this origin) adds a random `w=<16 hex>` group token to the hash, opens the path on the current selection and joins a `BroadcastChannel` named for the token; the other window opens on that hash and joins the same channel, each local change travels once as a delta, a message that is not a valid selection is ignored, and a received change is never sent back. The Network pop-out is `/flows/network/` (`networkPage`, controller `assets/js/flows-popout.js`), opened by the Market Network tab's Pop out button; the Now list's pop-out is `/flows/now/` (`nowPage`, controller `assets/js/flows-now.js`). The test is Chromium with `page.route` and no server.

### `assets/js/flows-popout.js`, `assets/css/flows-popout.css`, `tests/flows-popout-render.mjs`

The Network pop-out, `/flows/network/` (`networkPage` in `shared/flows-pages.js`, a member page in the one page array with no gate; a bare path is a 308 and a signed-out reader gets the sign-in page): a title row, the freshness pill and one module `#pnNet` filling the rest of the viewport, one screen with no page scroll at 1440x820, 1024x768 and 390x844 (`assets/css/flows-popout.css`, which also styles the Market Network tab's Pop out button, 44 px on a coarse pointer, hidden while `FlowsUI.link.popout` is absent). `assets/js/flows-popout.js` (`FlowsUI.popout`) reads `/api/flows/flowalerts` every 60 s and sooner when the rail's `fl` topic speaks, and `/api/flows/universe?cols=t,sec`, mounts `FlowsUI.netMap` through the `net` and `netmap` defer groups, and joins `FlowsUI.link`: a ticker or sector chosen in the opener selects its chip, a chip chosen here is emitted once, and a received selection is never sent back. A failed, unpublished or empty feed shows its reason, never a blank. `tests/flows-popout-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds the paint to the fixture's independent sums, the screen at three sizes, a BroadcastChannel peer, the three feed states, the Market button and three planted wrong builds.

### `assets/js/flows-now.js`, `assets/css/flows-now.css`, `tests/flows-now-render.mjs`

The Flagged now pop-out, `/flows/now/` (`nowPage` in `shared/flows-pages.js`, a member page in the one page array with no gate; a signed-out reader gets the sign-in page): a title row with a Back to Market link (`.mk-pop`) and one module `#pwList`. It fits one screen with no page scroll and no inner scroll at 1440x820, 1024x768 and 390x844. It loads `assets/css/flows-popout.css` for the shared pop-out layout and `.mk-pop`, plus `assets/css/flows-now.css`, which the Market page does not load. `assets/js/flows-now.js` reads `/api/flows/flowalerts` every 60 s, and sooner when the rail's `fl` topic speaks. It lists one row per flagged window, largest premium first, with a window that has no premium last: ticker (a link to `/flows/ticker/?t=`), call or put, strike, expiry, premium, lean and the window's start in TRT. The lean follows the convention: calls at the ask and puts at the bid are bullish, calls at the bid and puts at the ask are bearish, and the rest are unsided. A side counts when it holds more than half the premium. An absent field is an em dash with its reason in the cell's title. The list shows the rows that fit and a `+N more` line for the rest, and refits on resize. It joins `FlowsUI.link` for tickers: a row chosen by click or Enter is emitted once, a ticker chosen in the opener highlights its rows, and a received selection is never sent back. A failed, unpublished or empty feed shows its reason, and the count is an em dash, never a zero. `tests/flows-now-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) checks the rows against an independent sort of the fixture, checks the lean and the TRT clock on a hand-built set, and covers the screen at three sizes, a BroadcastChannel peer in both directions, the three feed states, the back link and three planted wrong builds.

### `tests/flows-weight.mjs`, `tests/flows-defer-render.mjs`

`FlowsUI.defer(group, {when, el, label})` loads a group of scripts after the page's `load` event and returns a promise that resolves when they have run. A group is declared in the page as one `<i hidden data-defer="<group>" data-src="${v(path)}">` element per file (a list in one `data-src` is refused: the version scan cannot see past its first URL) and runs in declared order, one file after another: the next is requested only when the one before it has run, and the first failure stops the group, so a file never runs ahead of one it follows and a retry resumes at the file that failed. `when: "idle"` (default) waits for `requestIdleCallback` with a 1.5 s timeout; `when: "visible"` waits for `el` to come within 200 px of the viewport, then idle. Under `navigator.connection.saveData` the slot (`el`) gets a status line and a button and nothing is fetched until it is pressed; a failed fetch removes its script element, requests nothing further from the group and puts an unavailable line with Retry above the slot's own content, and the promise stays pending until a retry works. A second call for a group returns the first promise. `flows-ui.js` has one script loader, `loadJs`, shared by `defer` and the `FlowsUI.link` stub; `flows-dock.js` has the only other. The Unusual page loads `flows-net.js` this way (`defer("net", {when: "visible", el: #uaNet, label})`): the host is `.fn-host.is-slot` in the served HTML, so its height is reserved at first paint by container-query bands in `flows-net.css` (the mounted network's height at each card width, measured; the header's two-row wrap under 540 px too). When the alerts arrive empty, withheld, pending or failed, `flows-unusual.js` swaps `is-slot` for `.is-quiet`, whose own bands are the height of the network's empty state (about 450 to 790 px against 1,000 to 1,360 for the full scene), so the card is not a one-line message in a 1,100 px box once the network has mounted and nothing below it moves when it does; inside the Unusual page's one-screen stage `.fd-stage` instead lets the quiet host fill its own card and reserves nothing, because an absolutely placed card has nothing below it to move; a feed with rows swaps back. Until the module mounts it holds a table of the eight largest names by flagged premium with their lean by the convention, written by `flows-unusual.js` from the alerts it already has. `flows-defer-render` holds that nothing below the card moves when the network arrives, that the reservation never falls short of the mounted height by more than 6 px nor leaves more than 50 px empty at eleven widths on a fresh load, in the full scene and in the empty one (an empty feed and a 500 at 1440 and 390 px move nothing below the card, with a layout shift under 0.1), that a two-file group whose first file fails once requests nothing of the second and runs both in order after Retry, that Data Saver and a 404 leave the table in place, and that the request starts after the load event. `tests/flows-weight.mjs` weighs a route as its eager scripts plus the files `DEFERRED` lists for it (declared groups), the dock's `flows-ask.js` and the `flows-link.js` body, against a stated per-route allowance; it scans every `flows-*.js` for a second loader and for a `.defer("group")` call the route's `DEFERRED` entry does not list, runs the declaration checker on eight negative fixtures (a space and a comma in `data-src`, a file missing from `DEFERRED`, a table entry with no element on the page, an unversioned `data-src`, an element that is not hidden, a file that is also a script src, a file other than the table's), and prints the headroom of each route against the 1 KiB floor with the pending `EAGER_KERNEL` entries subtracted on live routes: the routes under the floor are the `BELOW_FLOOR` list, each with the row that relieves it, and the list must equal the computed set in both directions. The ticker's ceiling carries a named `TICKER_RAISE` (OD-08, 1,613 B, the cost of `FlowsUI.defer` on a route that defers no group): it is held at that size, asserted to be deleted as soon as the ticker defers as many bytes of group files as it was raised by, and printed on every run; the kernel entries state their size and a landed entry is measured against it (`FlowsUI.defer` 1.6 KB, so the V and L share of the kernel is held at 3.8 KB under the named `KERNEL_LIMIT` decision, not the 3.1 KB of section 2.10). `flows-defer-render` is Chromium with `page.route` and no server.

### `assets/js/flows-overview-below.js`, `tests/flows-home-below-render.mjs`, `tests/lib/below-fold.mjs`

The Home page's deferred chunk (F-08 part a). `flows-overview.js` keeps the first-paint modules and the data reads; the painters of What changed, Volatility, Reporting and Headlines (`paintChanged`, `paintVol`, `paintEvents`, `paintNews`, `renderSpine`) are in this file, declared as `<i hidden data-defer="home-below">`, asked for with `FlowsUI.defer("home-below", {when: "visible", el: #hmChg})` and handed the helpers they use through `FlowsUI.homeKit` (the first-paint file publishes it before the chunk runs; the chunk calls `homeKit.attach` with the five painters and `homeKit.attached` says it has). A paint asked for before the chunk lands is held by key in `later()` and replayed from the current data when it does; one asked for afterwards runs at once. The bento puts What changed on the first screen at the xl, l and m fits, so the request is made after the load event and after the hero's first paint; at the s fit it waits for the Glance page of the pager and the other three pages request nothing. Until the chunk lands, Volatility, Reporting and Headlines show their own pending state and Reporting's numeral is a dash with that reason. The render test is Chromium and `page.route`, no server: no request before the load event, none at 390x844 before Glance opens, the hero and first-paint modules painted before the request, data before chunk and chunk before data, a 404 with Retry, Data Saver, 390 px, and the lower tiles' strip, compact and glance faces. The browser suites that read the lower modules open their pages through `revealBelow(browser)` (`tests/lib/below-fold.mjs`), whose flatten stylesheet turns the stage into a block of full-width tiles, makes the chunk's trigger fire at once and holds `page.goto` until it has attached.

### `assets/js/flows-ticker-surface.js`, `tests/flows-surface-render.mjs`, `tests/lib/surface-card.mjs`

The ticker's Surface & terrain view, the second tab of the chart panel (`FlowsUI.tickerSurface`: `mount` and the pure `model.surface` and `model.terrain`), declared as `<i hidden data-defer="surface" id="ftSurface">` when `FLOWS_SCENES` is `on` (or `owner` and the member is an owner) and loaded by `FlowsUI.defer` when the tab opens. It replaces the old scenes module and the dock's Volatility surface and Gamma terrain buttons. Rows are the union of the gamma grid's expiries and the engine's, joined by `FlowsUI.tickerGlance.model.expiries`, so the chip row, the strike rail on the price view and these scenes read one list; a chip carries the engine's fit word (FITTED SVI, SVI, REPAIRED or NO FIT). Terrain: dealer gamma by strike and expiry as columns, heights to the 98th percentile (clipped at 1.25 times it, marked), a cell the vendor did not return a dashed outline and never a zero. Surface: the engine's stored SVI read through `FlowsQuant.sliceTotalVariance` in k = ln(K/F), total variance interpolated linearly in time at a fixed strike between consecutive fitted expiries (`between`), rows that span an expiry with no fit faded, an expiry with no fit a ring at its at-the-money level and never a curve, and a calendar check that scans total variance against time. Both share one camera and it orbits: pointer drag turns yaw (within 55 degrees) and pitch (8 to 60) and a flick glides on with inertia that decays out in under a second and stops at once on a touch; the wheel and a two-finger pinch zoom between 0.7 and 2.4; arrow keys, plus and minus, Home and a double click work from the keyboard and mouse; the Front, Side, Top and Reset buttons and Home glide the camera on an eased 520 ms path; an Auto toggle drifts the yaw gently once the reader has been still for 1.8 s, and it is off under reduced motion, in a hidden tab and out of view; a touch drag turns the scene sideways while `touch-action: pan-y` leaves vertical swipes to the page. One loop runs only while something moves (`data-motion` says moving, paused or rest, and the paint census is zero at rest). A fresh mount grows the bars and the mesh from the floor (640 ms, staggered by depth), an expiry change raises the chosen row, an update (`update(card)`, same shape only) tweens heights and implied vols from the old values over 520 ms, hover lights the column and row under the pointer with a rim, a lit crosshair and a floating readout whose figure counts up in 360 ms, and depth fog and a specular sheen shade the faces. Every settled frame is the frame a fresh reduced-motion mount draws, pixel for pixel, and the readout lands on the cell's exact value. Mesh vertices, cell geometry and the lookup table of colours are cached on the model and drawn from typed arrays, so a frame of both scenes costs about 2 ms and allocates tens of kilobytes. Strike, expiry and level labels go through one declutter pass that drops what would overlap and records every box, canvases are described with a table fallback, and on a phone the three columns are swipeable cards. A card with neither a smile nor a gamma grid shows the withheld reason. `tests/flows-surface-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds the strike rail to the price axis within 1 px, the expiry cursor and its one-sigma band, 25-delta strikes, risk reversal and butterfly to an independent solve and to the engine's stored figures, null cells, thinned time-axis labels, the tab and keyboard, the calendar check, withheld states, the orbit, glide, zoom, auto-rotate, hover, tween, census, cost and touch checks and twenty-five planted wrong builds. `tests/lib/surface-card.mjs` builds its synthetic card.

### `assets/js/flows-ticker-glance.js`, `tests/flows-glance-render.mjs`

The ticker glance's hero chart and six instrument cards, attached as `FlowsUI.tickerGlance` (`mount(card, hosts, opts)` and the pure `model(card, range, extra)` with `layout`, `cluster`, `cards`, `readout`, `describe`, `claims` and `evidence` beside it). `mount` fills two hosts, `ftGlanceChart` and `ftGlanceCards`, and returns `{ setRange, setExpiry, expiry, attach, update, destroy }` (`attach(hook)` is the seam the deferred live layer binds through); `opts` is `{ reading, summary, range }` plus `cardX` and `tape`, the two payloads the card alone does not carry (the dossier companion for earnings, short volume, insiders, the cone and NOPE, and the live tape for the session's cumulative premium), and `update({ reading, claims, cardX, tape, card })` repaints. The chart is one SVG sized from its host: closes for 20, 63, 126 or 252 sessions back, today, and 20 sessions forward in which Q (the card's implied range, orange, solid edges) and P (its realised range, blue, dashed) widen with the square root of time from the variance per session each range implies, both stepped at the report (half a session after the close for an after-close report, before for a pre-open one) by one standard deviation of the report move: for Q the term structure's event sd, else the quoted expected move times sqrt(pi / 2); for P the root mean square of the last twelve one-day report moves less one ordinary session of the realised variance, which needs four reports and otherwise takes no step; and no step when the report is not inside the horizon; the call wall, put wall, max pain, flip and spot as lines with right-edge labels placed by one clustering rule (labels merge into a stack a label height apart, centred on the mean of their levels, held inside the plot); the gamma dealers added today by strike as bars scaled to the largest, blue long and hatched orange short, under a header with the book figure on the vendor's convention; the Flows score by session under the price on one scale, today's bar emphasised; and a legend row that names, instead of drawing, every layer the card cannot supply. Forward dates (the readout and the axis labels) are counted over the sixty exchange sessions the ticker page carries in a hidden element `#ftCal`, written at render time from `shared/flows-quant-time.js` by `sessionCalendar` in `shared/flows-pages.js`, and fall back to weekends only past the end of that list. The chart is one tab stop whose arrow keys, Home, End and Escape move a crosshair across the sessions with the reading in a polite live region; its accessible description carries the close, both moves, the report, the levels and the dealer clause. The six cards (Signal, Volatility, Flow, Positioning, Earnings, Range) take each figure from the same card fields the lower modules read, show an em dash with the reason in the footer line when the input is absent, and become 68 px tiles of a title, a figure and a unit below 641 px of container width. Linking is a document event, `ft:focus` with `{ id, source }` (`mark`, `card` or `claim`): with a reading, claims are numbered in reading order (the `now` section, the drivers, the tensions, then the watch list, at most four, or `opts.claims`), `EVIDENCE` maps fact ids to layers and cards by prefix, marks stand on the first layer a claim evidences (flip, report, gamma, walls, cone), every layer and card declares `data-claims`, and a focused claim keeps its evidence and steps everything else back; with no reading there are no marks, no `data-claims` and no dimming. Every colour is a class on a token under the `.ftg-` rules of `flows-ticker.css`. `tests/flows-glance-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds the cone, the step, the labels, the bars and every card figure to independent computations on the dry-run emit's real card, the absent-input states, the range control, the dimming, keyboard, sizing at 1440, 1920, 390 and 320 px, reduced motion, and thirteen planted wrong builds. Strike rail (current): when the card carries expiries, the right of the chart is no longer the aggregate gamma profile but a rail on the same price axis (`model.expiries`, `defaultExpiry`, `skew`, `miniRail`): dealer gamma by strike for the chosen expiry (a cell not returned is a dashed outline, never a zero), the smile with 25-delta put and call markers and the quotes the card holds, a one-sigma band of spot times exp(plus or minus the expiry's at-the-money volatility times the square root of T), and clickable expiry ticks on the forward axis (a radio group; labels thinned so none overlap the today label) that move the expiry cursor and announce `ft:expiry`. Under about 600 px the rail drops below as an expiry chip row and two mini charts. The 25-delta strikes come from `FlowsQuant.skewMetrics`, exported by the read bundle with `sliceTotalVariance`.

### `assets/js/flows-ticker-live.js`, `tests/flows-glance-live-render.mjs`

The ticker glance's live layer, the deferred group `live`. `tickerPage` always declares `<i hidden data-defer="live" id="ftLive">`; `flows-ticker.js` connects the rail for `px`, `fl` and `gx` naming its own ticker and asks for the group with `FlowsUI.defer("live")` only in the regular session, on the first streamed quote or the first heartbeat that says `rth`, so a closed market never fetches it. The file adds `FlowsUI.tickerLive` (`start`, `state`, and the pure `model.geom`, `pathOf`, `sentence`, `flagged` and `flowFoot`) and binds to the mounted glance through `mount.attach(hook)`, the one seam `flows-ticker-glance.js` opens: the hook's `input` hands `model` the live price (`last`, which turns the right-edge label from Spot to Last and widens the y domain by the same 6 per cent pad only when the price leaves it) and a sticky domain (`dom`, never shrinking within one card and range), `drawn` and `cards` run after each full paint, `said` extends the chart's description, and `update` patches a session flow refresh into the Flow card in place. The price is drawn only while the rail's `px` entry is live by its own state (`FlowsUI.freshFrom` over `FlowsUI.rt.fresh("px")`, read again at the end of its live window and whenever the pill changes state); a stale, lagging or absent quote draws nothing, never a zero, and the description says why. In the today slot (from the card close to the next session, the session's own minutes in TRT from `F.open` over 390) it draws a dashed connector from the card close, the intraday path (points at least 10 s apart, at most 720, a gap over 150 s breaks it), a halo and a dot, turns the card-close dot into a hollow anchor and the axis date to the live session, and prints `Last <price> · HH:MM TRT` in the legend row; the today marker does not move. Each tick writes only the attributes that changed on the same elements; the svg is rebuilt only when the price leaves the domain or the quote stops being live. `gx` prints `Dealer γ now <$> per 1%` with the vendor's convention clause in its title and in the description, apart from the nightly gamma; `fl` rows the page already holds add `flagged <n> <$>` to the Flow card's foot. The pill prints `Live · <s> s behind the vendor` (the hub's read minus the quote's own stamp, hidden under 900 px). A heartbeat quote older than the streamed one keeps the streamed price, so the hero and the chart agree. No new request, payload, vendor or model call, no frame loop and no animation. `tests/flows-glance-live-render.mjs` (Chromium, `page.route` and `page.routeWebSocket`, a fake clock, no server) serves the real ticker page against the dry-run emit with rail frames and holds the dot to the model's own scale at every tick, the label, stamp, hero and pill, the same svg and path elements with zero removed nodes and no layout shift, zero writes at rest, the gx and fl patches, a session flow refresh that touches only the Flow card, one repaint when the price leaves the domain, the stale, silent and absent feeds, the requests made, the return to the closed look, reduced motion, 390 and 320 px, a closed market that never fetches the chunk, and eight planted wrong builds.
