### `tests/truth/*.mjs`, `tests/flows-truth.mjs`, `tests/rt-market-sim.mjs`, `tests/gen-truth-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-truth.json`

The one synthetic market the statistics are judged on. `rng.mjs` is sfc32 seeded from sha256 (equal word for word to the Python twin in the generator script); `worlds.mjs` holds W1 (the production GARCH-skew-t class), W1d (the dependent daily world), W3 and W3b (marked Hawkes flow, negative-binomial flow counts with day effects and weather), W4 (look-ahead bait), W5 (open-interest lag), W6 (overfitting null), W7 (calibration worlds) and W8 (overlapping horizons); `intraday.mjs` is W9, the 670-name minute world, and W9o, its out-of-family preset; `runner.mjs` refuses to accept a result from a well-specified cell and to judge with no out-of-family world; `reference.mjs` holds the reference implementations the planted wrong builds are made from; `mutants.mjs` loads a copy of a module through a data URL with exact-once string transforms (production is never edited) and `detectors.mjs` holds the detector each mutant must fail and the correct build must pass. `flows-truth.mjs` prints the kill matrix; `FLOWS_TRUTH_DEEP=1` runs ten times the replications on fresh seeds and prints `FLAG` lines without failing. `rt-market-sim.mjs` serves one seeded event log as the vendor's REST routes and its socket protocol on a virtual clock, with the chaos knobs (drops, stalls, duplicates, reorders within 500 ms, malformed frames, caps, 429, lag spikes, clock skew, a silent channel, a missing periscope frame). The fixtures' provenance records the numpy and scipy versions and the sha256 of the script, and the suite fails when the script changed without a regenerate. `W1_BANDS` and the `w1-jumps-production-clip-in-filter` expectation pin today's winsorising limit (K 6): a change to it (F-22, or P3-16's K 6 to 10, which moves the measured tail ratio to about 0.965) fails `w1-bands` and the baseline of the `garchRecovery` mutants, and must re-pin `W1_BANDS` and flip that expectation in the same change. Most planted wrong builds mutate `reference.mjs` or `detectors.mjs`, not production code; each later row retargets its mutants at the production path it adds, one line in `mutants.mjs`.

### `tests/flows-alert-grid.mjs`, `tests/truth/alert-reference.mjs`, `tests/truth/alert-grid.mjs`, `tests/truth/alert-detectors.mjs`, `tests/truth/alert-mutants.mjs`, `tests/truth/alertsim-evidence.mjs`

The alert decision grid (F-14), the acceptance contract every alert construction is judged by. `alert-reference.mjs`: BY with N fixed at the open on running p that only fall (so the union over a session is the BY set at the close), the null-cell bank, and the two registered constructions with the same 1, 5, 15 and 60 minute windows (A, a calibrated session-maximum law read through the cell nu-hat minus 1 and 1.5 s-hat; B, a per-increment transformed normal mixture, the overnight gap included, scaled by e to the c s-hat). `alert-grid.mjs`: the cells (the exact world; heterogeneous tails with scale error 0.1, 0.2 and 0.3 and random-sign sectors; the overnight-gap world; W9o with its nightly measurement and paused buckets), covered and report-only steps, the session-clustered FDR bound, the J x D power cells and their pre-registered weights, the BH challenger on residualised p, the macro-day stress cell and `decide()`, the registered rule. `alertsim-evidence.mjs` carries the alertsim4 and alertsim3 outputs the rule is also run on. `alert-mutants.mjs` plants five wrong builds through `mutants.mjs` and `alert-detectors.mjs` holds the detector each must fail. `FLOWS_TRUTH_DEEP=1` runs 670 names on fresh seeds and prints `FLAG` lines without failing.

### `tests/gen-har-fixtures.py`, `tests/fixtures-har.json`

### `tests/flows-pages-render.mjs`, `tests/fixtures-pages-render-baseline.json`

Every page function in `FLOWS_PAGES` (fifteen) rendered in Chromium at 320, 390 and 1440 px against the pipeline's dry-run emit, served from `page.route` with no server (about 2.5 minutes, 60 s of it the emit). Hard assertions: one `h1`, no skipped heading level, no page error, and the Ask dock (a dialog with `aria-modal`, nothing outside it focusable, focus inside and the main region `inert` when narrow; a complementary landmark beside a usable page when wide; Escape closes and focus returns to the tab). Counted against the committed baseline, which may fall but not rise: axe-core `color-contrast`, unnamed controls, structure rules, horizontal-overflow pixels, targets under 24 px, and (coarse pointer) targets under 44 px. `PAGES_RENDER_UPDATE=1` rewrites the baseline; the metrics go to `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`; no screenshot is kept. `axe-core` is pinned in `tests/package.json`; where `tests/node_modules` predates the pin, `NODE_PATH` can point at a directory that has it.

### `tests/flows-deadline-render.mjs`

The Flows fetch wrapper's deadline (`bounded` in `assets/js/flows-ui.js`): every GET to `/api/flows/*` that carries no signal of its own is aborted at 20 s, or at its `deadlineMs`, and the timer stays armed until `json()`, `text()`, `blob()` or `arrayBuffer()` settles, so a body that stalls after its headers is bounded too; a caller's signal wins, a POST and any other URL are untouched, and a 401 still hangs. Home and Ask pass `deadlineMs: 15000`, the desk and strategy 45000. Chromium against a loopback HTTP server that hangs, or stalls after headers, and a paused page clock stepped with `runFor`: twelve pages each show a failure state, and a source scan holds every `AbortSignal.timeout` and `.any` in `assets/js` behind `?.`. A page test that jumps the fake clock past 20 s with a fetch in flight now aborts it, so such tests step the clock in 10 s pieces.

### `tests/flows-read-failures.mjs`, `tests/flows-meter-worker.mjs`

Two small suites for the measured half of the data backbone. `flows-read-failures` holds the failed-is-not-empty rule: a card built with an empty failure list is byte for byte the card built with none, a failed read marks the panel it feeds `unavailable` with code `failed` and a reason that names the route and the HTTP status or the timeout, a panel no failed read feeds is untouched, `uw()` reaches its caller with `status` on every HTTP failure (404, a 503 that outlasts its retries, and a 429 that exhausts them) and as a timeout or a parse failure otherwise, the meter counts each attempt in the class it landed in, the nightly deadline is 30 s, 45 s for an option-contracts page and the two-year bars, and `FLOWS_UW_NIGHTLY_DEADLINE=off` restores the unbounded client, all against a loopback vendor. `flows-meter-worker` drives `worker.js` with `FLOWS_METER` off, on, malformed and unset: no timing header and an unmetered `uwFetch` unless the word is `on`, `Server-Timing: total;dur` on API answers (errors and 401s included) and never on a page, and the Worker's vendor meter counting a chain read and a vendor 503 as a 5xx, per instance.

### `tests/pipeline-sim/*.mjs`, `tests/flows-pipeline-chaos.mjs`

The deterministic pipeline simulator and its invariant harness. `tests/pipeline-sim/clock.mjs` is a virtual clock that replaces `setTimeout`, `clearTimeout`, `Date.now` and `AbortSignal.timeout` and drives a promise by firing the earliest timer whenever nothing else can run, so the real `uw()` client, with its permit queue, backoff, `Retry-After` waits and deadlines, runs thousands of faulted calls in milliseconds; a promise that no timer can ever settle is reported as hung. `vendor.mjs` is a fake vendor behind `fetch` that serves the registered operations from a per-path script of faults (ok, a clean empty answer, 404 and 5xx, a 429 with `Retry-After`, a stall that never answers, a slow answer, a body that is not JSON) and refuses any origin but its own; `world.mjs` seeds one sfc32 stream from sha256 and keeps a trace. Eight worlds are built on them: `client` (the real `uw()` against scripted faults: attempts, results and failure kinds match an independent statement of the retry rules, no call outlives its bound, the meter's count, classes and exact failure codes equal what the vendor answered and every failure sample carries only its code, time, page and attempt, the rate floor stays inside its range), `cards` (the real `readDeepFeeds` into `buildCard`: the failed reads are exactly the scripted ones, the panels they feed are code failed and no other panel changes, a healthy card is byte for byte unchanged, every card validates), `cards-plan` (the same with the failures handed in), `store` (a night's keys with crashes, stale carries, absent and unsessioned keys, skewed sessions, cut and unreadable listings, stale globals: the verifier's states, counts, predicates, completeness and ordering equal a restatement from the world's own bookkeeping, conservation holds, no false green and no false alarm, and its frozen inputs are never written), `contracts` (planted corruptions refused by code and path, synthetic names refused outside a test, canonical form independent of key order and stamps), `meter` and `stages` (the codes, samples, the failure summary and its byte budget, and each stage's failure line), and `volflow` (`volflow.mjs`: the vol-flow stage on the virtual clock, the real `runFlowLeg` and `uw()` at the 150 ms pace and the real store's `hold`, `publish` and `flushHeld` against a store that answers in 2 s, with scripted multi-leg pages (short, full, a refused later page, a refused or failing first page) and refused card-x writes: every held vol section lands, a name whose write lands is written once and never vol-only first, a merged write refused once is written again and one refused for good falls back to the held payload, the multi-leg tally and the meter's codes equal the script, and the flow leg's time stays within 1.2 times its vendor calls at the pace plus 2 s and 6 s a failing chain, which an inline write on the lane breaks). `tests/flows-pipeline-chaos.mjs` runs all eight for 300 seeds (`volflow` on every tenth) or, with `FLOWS_TRUTH_DEEP=1`, 20,000, prints a table, and on a violation prints the seed, the first broken invariant and the last events, with the command that replays it. The door's own invariants (a write never replaces a newer, better or earlier-session row, a refused write leaves the last good row byte for byte) wait for the door that enforces them; nothing here models it.

### `tests/truth/pipeline-detectors.mjs`, `tests/truth/pipeline-mutants.mjs`, `tests/flows-pipeline-mutants.mjs`

The pipeline's kill matrix. `tests/truth/pipeline-mutants.mjs` plants 61 wrong builds the way `alert-mutants.mjs` does, each an exact-once string transform of a production module loaded through a data URL, never an edit: no deadline on the nightly client, the long deadline missing, timeouts retried forever, a clean empty answer thrown as an error, a 404 read as empty, a 429 exhaustion with no status, an unreadable body left out of the meter, a failure code dropped from a panel, a healthy card carrying an empty failure list, a darkpool failure on the wrong panel, the stale comparison reversed, a report that is complete with a key missing, a cut listing read as a failure, an unreadable listing read as ok, a verifier that writes to its inputs, synthetic names allowed, the canonical form keeping stamps or key order, a card for another ticker accepted, a literal route filed under its template, a refusal filed as a client error, a stage that reports the run's wire time, a parse failure metered as a network one, an uncapped error backoff, a failure filed by its class, a summary that names only the slowest failures, a stage that does not name its failures, a flow write on the lane, a failed later multi-leg page counted as a first, a held vol payload written at once, a flush that drops an unlanded key or never falls back. `tests/truth/pipeline-detectors.mjs` holds one detector for each target (the vendor client, `flows-card.js`, `flows-coverage.js`, `flows-contracts.js`, the call meter, the stage runner, `flows-deepen.js`, and `volflowDetector` for the flow leg, the store and the client's backoff), each running its simulator world over seeded runs; Each baseline is loaded as its own module instance (`loadMutated`'s `salt`), so one detector's run leaves no pace in another's. `tests/flows-pipeline-mutants.mjs` requires that every detector passes the correct build and fails every mutant meant for it.

### `tests/flows-oracle-core.mjs`, `tests/flows-oracle-render.mjs`, `tests/lib/oracle-fixtures.mjs`, `tests/lib/oracle-world.mjs`

The Neuron's client lane. `flows-oracle-core` loads the core in a bare `vm` and holds the formatters, every template and every detector threshold from both sides (at the bar and one dollar under it, at 64.4 and 64.6 per cent printed, 749 and 750 contracts, 19 and 20 minutes into the session) to independent computations, runs 400 random calls and every template through the Ask guard with modals on and the advice and lint lexicons, proves the Eastern arithmetic against the zone, and plants 28 wrong builds. `flows-oracle-render` (Chromium, `page.route`, no server) holds the sheet, the orb, the tab bar, the focus trap, the inert page, Escape and focus return at 1440, 1024, 390 and 320 px, the figures to an independent formatter, the dash for a null, TRT only, the resting state and Retry, the preference cookie, the glance block on a ticker rail, a scripted `fl` stream through the real detectors (messages, pill, count, throttled announcements, no message from a stale feed, a hostile ticker that never reaches `innerHTML`), reduced motion with no running animation, a zero paint census, a source scan for colour literals, comments, layout transitions and storage, and 12 planted wrong builds of the served files. `oracle-fixtures` builds records to the frozen contract with fixture tickers (`SYO001`...) and `oracle-world` serves a page and the routes.

### `tests/lib/virtual-clock.mjs`

A virtual frame clock for the Chromium suites, installed into a page that is at rest: it replaces `requestAnimationFrame`, `cancelAnimationFrame` and `performance.now` with a queue stepped by the test (`advance`, `until`, `restore`) and dispatches pointer events carrying an explicit `timeStamp` (`pointer`, `flick`), so a glide, a flick velocity, a tween or an auto-rotate delay is measured in frame time and never in wall milliseconds. `flows-surface-render` drives the camera, presets, tweens, hover count-up and auto-rotate through it; a gate that must stay on real time is held as a ratio to a reference workload (`tests/lib/cpu-budget.mjs`).

### `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs`

The push run's `reuse` job: finds the pull-request run that tested the pushed tree through its `tested-tree` commit status and the Actions API, and writes `reused`, which lets `fast` and the shards skip; every doubt is a full run.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs` | `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#STATUS_AUTHOR`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#TESTED_CONTEXT`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#WORKFLOW_PATH`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#createApi`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#decide`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#main`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#mergedPull`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#runIdOf`, `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs#runProves` | pipeline |

### `scripts/prepush.mjs`

The local gate before a push: `node scripts/prepush.mjs [--base=<ref>] [--jobs=n] [--list]` names the suites a change can break and runs them in parallel with the always-on set (`contracts`, `docs`, `flows-weight`, `architecture`, `run`, `lib-contract` and the five Worker-table suites `server-kernel`, `server-rt`, `server-pages`, `server-router` and `route-matrix`, which scan whole trees the import closure cannot see). It joins `node run.mjs --affected-list` with a table of path rules for the render suites that a stylesheet or page change reaches but the import closure does not (`flows-pages-render`, `flows-glass-render`, `flows-overview`, `flows-home-onescreen` and their neighbours), prints one line per suite and the last 25 lines of each failure, and exits 1 on any failure. It reads no network and no vendor; the sandbox needs `PLAYWRIGHT_BROWSERS_PATH` and, for workerd suites, `FLOWS_TEST_SANDBOX=1`.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/prepush.mjs` |  |  |

### `tests/lib/affected.mjs`, `tests/ci-affected.mjs`

Affected-suite selection for pull requests: `node run.mjs --affected <base-ref>` (from `tests/`) reads `git diff --name-only <base>...HEAD`, joins each changed file to the suites whose import closure (the registry's walker, static and literal dynamic imports) or `covers` globs reach it, and runs those plus the whole `fast` group; `--affected-list` prints the selection without running, and with `--shard i/n` the shard keeps only its affected suites. Any doubt selects everything: an unreadable or empty diff, a file that maps to no suite (a deletion included), a file under `tests/lib/`, `tests/run.mjs`, the lockfile and `tests/package.json`, `.github/`, `wrangler*.toml`, `worker.js`, `.assetsignore` or `_headers`, or a file that reaches more than 40% of the suites. The workflow's `plan` job runs the list on pull requests only; the push-to-main run, the weekly schedule and manual dispatch never select, and a partial pull-request run writes no `tested-tree` status, so `scripts/ci-reuse.mjs` can never skip the push gate on a partial run. `ci-affected` proves exact selections on a miniature repository (leaf, transitive, literal dynamic import, covers, registry file), every fallback, the real `git diff`, and that selectors ignoring `covers` or transitive imports fail the same expectations.

| File | Exports | Suites |
|---|---|---|
| `tests/ci-affected.mjs` |  | ci-affected |
| `tests/lib/affected.mjs` | `tests/lib/affected.mjs#FAN_OUT_LIMIT`, `tests/lib/affected.mjs#FULL_PATTERNS`, `tests/lib/affected.mjs#changedFiles`, `tests/lib/affected.mjs#describeSelection`, `tests/lib/affected.mjs#mapSuites`, `tests/lib/affected.mjs#rootOf`, `tests/lib/affected.mjs#selectAffected` | ci-affected, run |

### `wrangler.staging.toml`, `scripts/ops-probe.mjs`, `scripts/staging-load.mjs`, `tests/staging-contract.mjs`

The staging Worker's complete, separate configuration (own D1 and `PULSE`, namespaces 2101 to 2106, no cron, no route, no AI binding, live dispatch off, no secret); the external prober (`probe`: `/api/health` and one page, two failures to alert, a webhook from the environment) and the `smoke` checks; the 20-member poll and socket load test, which refuses production and prices a Free run first. `staging-contract` runs the configuration through wrangler's own reader and the tools against fakes, with no server. DEPLOY.md 10.6.
